**Ledger flips (1f49f45).** CV-43 and RP-13 FIXED at ee7b6e0: CI run 41976819752 on 2952958 ran the Dist job's `Run pnpm check:snippets` step green, which was the one clause the wave-1 judge left open on both rows (wave1-150-extras3.json through ledger-apply-wave.mjs).

**CI on the pushed head 111c351, and a process rule.** Run 41982245737 (06:42Z to 06:45Z) FAILED on two things, both W2-06 artefacts that anticipated W2-03's then-uncommitted code: the dry-run diff test in tests/unit/codemod-cli.test.ts expects the single-quoted import that only import-rewrite's printOptions print produces (HEAD's `root.toSource()` printed double quotes), and check:package's file list names dist/codemod/chunk-jscodeshift.mjs and chunk-print-options.mjs, which rolldown splits out only when a transform imports print-options.js and jscodeshift. A throwaway worktree at 111c351 with its own offline install (run dir head-probe-111c351.log) reproduced both: its dist/codemod holds chunk-worker.mjs, cli.mjs, worker.mjs and the transforms, and the test fails 1 of 62. Neither expectation was weakened; W2-03 (58302f4) supplies the code, and the chunk set is re-checked with `pnpm build && pnpm check:package` before the next push. Rule from here: a partial-wave commit set is verified at HEAD, away from the shared checkout's uncommitted sibling work, before any push (scratchpad head-check.sh: worktree at HEAD, offline install, vitest, removed; `git worktree remove` fails with 'Filename too long' on the worktree's node_modules, so the script deletes with rm -rf and prunes). After the landing the pre-push gates ran on the clean tree at 07:40:50Z (run dir push-150-w2-final.log): Biome 313 files in 106 ms, the typecheck script over modern-cmdk, create-modern-cmdk, tests, tests/e2e and tests/docs, size-limit with all seven entries under budget; lefthook's pre-push hook then built the packages and ran vitest and size-limit again (its summary: done in 11.94 seconds, build 0.59, test 10.44, size 0.21); the push landed 111c351..ffe6bb1; CI run 41988965827 on ffe6bb1 ran 07:41:07Z to 07:45:11Z and completed SUCCESS (ci-watch-ffe6bb1.log), the dry-run diff test and the tarball file list green with W2-03's code in place. The pre-commit hook was never bypassed: commit-wave.mjs runs a plain git commit -F, lefthook 2.2.2 answers git hook run pre-commit, and pnpm typecheck takes 2.3 s under TypeScript 7's native compiler, which is why the four landing commits took about three seconds each (commit-w2-final.log step timestamps, 07:39:10Z to 07:39:22Z).

**Judge** (ledger-dispositions-150-wave2, run wf_761cd6e6-377: 3 opus-verifier groups, 431K subagent tokens, 6 min 26 s, judged at HEAD ffe6bb1 with the working tree equal to HEAD apart from the owner's untracked AGENTS.md). 66 rows over the eight items: FIXED 62, PARTIAL 4, OPEN 0, REFUTED 0; per item W2-01 20 FIXED, W2-02 14, W2-03 8, W2-04 7, W2-05 7 FIXED and 4 PARTIAL, W2-06 3, W2-07 1, W2-08 2. The four PARTIALs: DT-07, F31 and RP-23 on one sentence, packages/create-modern-cmdk/README.md:75 still said dependencies are declared as caret ranges of known-good versions while fab60a5 writes exact pins (W2-05 never touched the README); CV-25 because templates/shared/src/main.tsx and shared/vite.config.ts sit in no tsconfig (tests/tsconfig.json includes only */src/App.tsx), which the judge deferred to W4-04's scaffold tsc. Its probes: the built dist/codemod/cli.mjs data-attrs --dry-run over a scratch project holding .css, .scss, JSX in .js, .json and edge selectors (every file migrated as specified, the .json skipped, one report, exit 0); vitest on the three transform suites 333 of 333 and on data-attrs 59 of 59; the create, pool, submodule, files, snippet, manifest and dist suites re-run singly, all green; pnpm view dist-tags.latest for the eight scaffold pins, each equal to versions.ts today; ATTR_MAP and UNMAPPED_ATTRS against cmdk 1.1.1's dist (13 rendered names, matched); import-rewrite --dry-run at concurrency 4 and 1 byte-identical. Two corrections of record from the judge: the fab60a5 commit message says the templates import modern-cmdk/react/styles.css while the code imports modern-cmdk/styles.css, the correct export; and RP-24's Command-root ref question belongs to RA-32, not to forward-ref. It proposed eight extra rows: three were documentation lines the wave's code had made false, fixed in 4ad3040 instead of filed (the README sentence above; data-attrs.ts's header now lists .mts and .cts in the script pass, which files.ts routes to the ts parser; forward-ref.ts's header now says a props spread in any single-parameter render function, typed or untyped, forwards the caller's ref, the typed form without a ref type argument reported, the untyped form unwrapped without a report), and five are filed below.

The new package builds dist/{cli,worker}.mjs, the print-options and worker chunks and transforms/*.mjs (8 files, 91.57 kB), bundles nothing but its own source (tsdown deps.neverBundle jscodeshift, onlyBundle [], onlyImport jscodeshift: the worker's probe with a stray `import 'ast-types'` failed the build with "ast-types is imported in chunk-print-options.mjs but is not included in deps.onlyImport option"), declares jscodeshift 17.4.0 as its one runtime dependency through `catalog:` (the verifier's `pnpm pack` shows 17.4.0 in the packed manifest), runs strict by default, carries a short true README stub (W7-02 writes the real one), MIT LICENSE, version 0.0.0 until changesets names it (W8-01), engines.node >=26.4.0. modern-cmdk keeps the codemod it ships until 2.0: tsdown.config.ts bundles ../modern-cmdk-codemod/src into dist/codemod/cli.mjs with deps.alwaysBundle jscodeshift, an explicit deps.onlyBundle list of ten packages (dropping tslib from the list fails the build: "tslib is located in node_modules but is not included in deps.onlyBundle option") and deps.onlyImport [] (node: builtins only), so the tarball needs no runtime dependency; its `bin` is dist/codemod/bin.mjs, the deprecated `modern-cmdk` shim (897 B): one deprecation line on stderr, --lenient put first unless argv carries --strict (the 1.x exit codes), the bundled cli.mjs run with program 'modern-cmdk'. **Ruling: recast stays 0.23.21, held (peak-modern §1).** The brief said: if W1-01's probe shows recast 0.24.0 keeps what printOptions relies on, add a pnpm override and run the whole fixture suite on it. The probe did show that, so the brief's condition picked the override; the worker chose a hold instead and did not run the suite on 0.24.0, and the verifier flagged the deviation as its first minor. The hold stands on a reason the brief had not weighed: jscodeshift 17.4.0 declares recast ^0.23.11 (pnpm view, 2026-11-05; latest is 0.24.0), and a pnpm override never reaches modern-cmdk-codemod's published manifest, so consumers would run recast 0.23.x while this repository's fixtures ran 0.24.0. A test suite that proves a version no consumer gets proves the wrong thing. .peak-modern-holds.json carries the entry (kind npm, name recast, pin 0.23.21, scoped to the lockfile) with that evidence; it is re-tested when jscodeshift's range moves. `pnpm why recast`: one version, 0.23.21, through jscodeshift 17.4.0 and @types/jscodeshift 17.3.0.

**W3-01b (e7bf0ce): the CLI's default profile follows the package it ships in.** The verifier's second minor: packages/modern-cmdk/dist/codemod/cli.mjs, run directly, used the strict modern-cmdk-codemod profile (Usage: modern-cmdk-codemod) while --version printed modern-cmdk 1.2.3, since only the shim passed program 'modern-cmdk'. Ruling: without CliDeps.program the profile follows the nearest package.json, which run() already located for --version (findPackageJSON from node:module); readNameAndVersion became readPackage, read once when no program is given; a package named modern-cmdk gives the lenient 'modern-cmdk' profile, anything else 'modern-cmdk-codemod'; the shim keeps passing 'modern-cmdk' explicitly (its contract; it needs no package.json, which the no-node_modules copy test relies on). The header and the CliDeps.program doc comment state the rule. Applied by a sonnet-worker through the Agent tool (92 s): tests/dist/cli-smoke.test.ts gains the bundled file as a lenient bin and two W3-01b titles that read both package.json files; 2 red first of 23 with cli.ts untouched, 23 green after the fix and a rebuild of both packages. **CI on 1223dc5 (run 41996249194, 08:40Z to 08:44Z): FAILED, one cause, fixed by W3-01d (2b36297).** Built-package tests: tests/dist/manifest.test.ts:120 expects dist/codemod/bin.mjs to carry an execute bit off Windows and found none on the ubuntu runner. The build did set it: the Build job's log reads "packages/modern-cmdk build: ℹ Granting execute permission to dist/codemod/bin.mjs" (and to cli.mjs, the codemod package's cli.mjs and create's dist/index.mjs; tsdown 0.23.5's ShebangPlugin chmods 0o755 in writeBundle for every entry chunk that starts with a shebang). The bit was lost between jobs: actions/upload-artifact's README ("Permission Loss", read 2026-11-05) says "File permissions are not maintained during zipped artifact upload. All directories will have `755` and all files will have `644`", and six jobs consume the dist artifact, the package job among them, so the published tarballs would have carried 644 bins as well (npm's bin links would still have made them executable on install). W3-01d: the Build job runs `tar -cf "$RUNNER_TEMP/dist.tar" packages/*/dist` and uploads that one file as the dist artifact (same pinned action, name, if-no-files-found and retention); dist-tests, e2e, size, bench, package and consumers download to runner.temp and run `tar -xf "$RUNNER_TEMP/dist.tar"` from the repo root; the node24-install job's tarballs artifact is untouched; the two comments that described the old shape and manifest.test.ts:119-120 state both facts. The README pairs tarring with `archive: false`; left at the default (a zip around the tar, unpacked by download-artifact) for W5-01 to weigh. The wave-4 and wave-5 briefs that touch ci.yml or release.yml (W4-02, W4-05, W5-01) and W4-01 carry a SINCE WAVE 3 paragraph with the tar shape, the package layout, the profile rule and the git resolution; W4-02 gains a tar-header mode check on every bin target when the tarball was packed on POSIX, which pins the chain end to end. **Security review of the 1223dc5 push, and W3-01c (cd9d415; SEC-57).** The automated review flagged files.ts (CWE-426, untrusted search path, MEDIUM): execFile('git', args, { cwd }) with the migrated repository as cwd. Ruling: resolve git to an absolute path from PATH alone, skipping empty and relative entries and any entry inside the target tree, and spawn that path (libuv searches nothing for an absolute path); the review's `-c core.fsmonitor=false` suggestion does not apply (a clone never carries the remote's .git/config). The worker's probe narrowed the finding honestly before changing anything: on this host (libuv 1.52.1, Node 28 nightly) a git.exe planted in the spawn cwd did not run (the real git answered, also when the process itself ran from that directory), while PATH set to `.;...` ran the planted copy, and an absolute PATH entry inside the tree would too. files.ts gained gitSearchDirs(pathValue, root) and resolveGit(root) (git.exe then git.com on win32, git elsewhere; fs.access X_OK, an existence check on Windows; memoized per root and PATH value; ENOENT naming the root when nothing is found, so locateRepo still maps a missing git to no-git); runGit spawns the result. tests/unit/codemod-files.test.ts, SEC-57 titles: nine gitSearchDirs rows, a planted root git (a regression guard: green on HEAD, matching the probe), a relative PATH entry (red on HEAD), an in-tree PATH entry, the ENOENT message; 12 red first of 58, then the files, submodule and CLI suites 124 passed, both packages' tsc and tests exit 0, Biome clean, the dist project 57 passed after a rebuild, the codemod CLI dry-run over the import-rewrite fixtures still "23 changed, 29 unchanged, 0 errors, 45 reported" (run dir w301c-gate.log; sonnet-worker, 172 s).

**CI on 2b36297 (run 41998415732, 08:57Z to 09:01Z): FAILED, four causes.** (1) Test on macos-26 and windows-2025, both Node lines (ubuntu passed): 'SEC-57: a PATH entry inside the repository is skipped and the real git answers' ran the planted copy during `git ls-files` ("git ls-files failed (exit 1): ... Cannot find module '/private/var/folders/.../ls-files'", and the long Temp path on Windows): locateRepo filtered PATH against the raw cwd (`/var/folders/...`, tmpdir() is a symlink on macOS; the runner's short TEMP path on Windows) and gitlinksOf against the realpath toplevel git prints, so the entry read as outside. Fixed by W3-01e, below. (2) Rust & WASM: wasm-pack failed to download binaryen version_130 from github.com (a GitHub 504 at 08:58Z; transient, nothing in the tree; green again on cdc05f2). (3) Links: lychee got a 504 Gateway Timeout from github.com on the repository's own issues URL (CONTRIBUTING.md:853; the same minute; transient). Both fragilities are now in W5-01's brief: install wasm-opt from a pinned binaryen release into a cached path instead of downloading on the hot path, and give lychee a retry budget for 5xx with the repository's own github.com URLs remapped or excluded. (4) Bundle Size: `pnpm size-limit --json` exited 1 with its report swallowed by the redirect; reproduced locally (run dir size-2b36297.json): modern-cmdk-codemod's own files 93,739 B against the 92,000 B budget, the SEC-57 code on a budget set 0.43 kB above W3-01's measurement. Ruled below.

**Security review of the cd9d415 push, and W3-01e (0a41f02; SEC-58).** The second review rated the residual HIGH (CWE-426): the trust boundary was the spawn cwd, so a subdirectory cwd or a submodule owner (ignoredIn passes the submodule directory) left the rest of the work tree searchable; PATH entries and candidates were not realpath-compared; a directory named git passed access. The same realpath gap is cause (1) of the CI failure above, so one fix closes both. Ruling: one trust boundary per operation, `trustBoundary(cwd)` = the realpath of the nearest directory from realpath(cwd) upward that holds a `.git` entry (what `rev-parse --show-toplevel` reports, computed without spawning git), else realpath(cwd); the nearest rather than the review's "outermost", because a dotfiles repository at the user's home would turn every PATH entry under the home (scoop shims, %LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Git\cmd, ~/.local/bin) into an excluded one and leave the user with no git. discoverFiles (strict profile) and findDirtyFiles compute it once and pass it to every runGit of the operation (rev-parse, status, ls-files, check-ignore in a submodule owner): cwd is where git runs, boundary is what PATH is filtered against. inside() holds the one containment test; gitSearchDirs stays the pure syntactic filter; resolveGit realpaths each kept entry (a dangling entry is skipped) and re-tests it; isGitOutside requires a regular file, X_OK and a realpath outside the boundary. Messages unchanged; the header and the doc comments state the rule and its reasons. Tests (SEC-58 titles; the SEC-57 titles stay): trustBoundary from a subdirectory, from a tree with no .git and through a symlink alias; a planted git in <repo>/bin with cwd a subdirectory; a PATH entry through a symlink alias of the repository; the CI shape (cwd the alias, so the raw cwd and git's toplevel differ; a junction on Windows, which needs no privilege); a directory named git on an outside entry; a planted git in the superproject's bin while check-ignore runs in the submodule (codemod-submodule.test.ts). 9 red first of 69 with files.ts untouched; then the files, submodule and CLI suites 132 passed, the CI command 1224 passed and 1 skipped, both packages' tsc and tests exit 0, Biome clean, the dist project 57 passed after a rebuild, the dry-run unchanged (sonnet-worker, 204 s); the orchestrator's gate on the merged tree: vitest 1281 passed and 1 skipped in 65 files, pnpm typecheck exit 0, Biome clean on the five touched files (run dir w301e-gate.log). The worker's one residual stands as written: trustBoundary realpaths cwd, so a nonexistent cwd now rejects ENOENT from discoverFiles or findDirtyFiles instead of reading as a missing git; cwd is process.cwd(). Severity: the review's HIGH is recorded; the ledger rates SEC-58 medium, since every path needs an absolute PATH entry inside the migrated tree or a link to it.

**Ruling: the modern-cmdk-codemod own-files budget is 100 kB (cdc05f2).** W3-01 set it at 92 kB over a 91.57 kB measurement; the SEC-57 code took the files to 93,739 B and the SEC-58 code to 96,307 B (`pnpm size-limit --json`, run dir size-w301e.json). The budget exists to catch a dependency bundled by mistake (jscodeshift's chunk alone is 3.05 MB in the modern-cmdk bin) and silent growth, not to refuse a security fix: 100 kB still fails on any bundled dependency and leaves 3.7 kB for 1.5.0's remaining codemod work. The other eight budgets are unchanged (core 6,399 of 6,500 B; react 5,558 of 5,600; dialog 1,422 of 1,500; CommandSeparator 4,830 of 5,000; core + react 10,019 of 10,100; styles.css 3,959 of 4,000; the codemod bin 3,148,577 of 3,400,000; the shim 897 of 1,000).

**Ruling: the pre-push size job's glob (a881dde; DT-67).** The pushes of 2b36297 and cdc05f2 both printed "verify ❯ size (skip) no matching push files" although each changed packages/modern-cmdk-codemod/src/files.ts: lefthook's glob `packages/*/src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` needs a directory between `src/` and `**/`, so files directly under a package's src never matched, and the budget failure above reached CI instead of the hook. Probed with `lefthook run pre-push --job size --file <path>`: files.ts skipped, codemod/bin.ts ran. The glob is now `{packages/*/src/**,packages/*/tsdown.config.ts,package.json}` (any file under a package's src, a tsdown config, or the root package.json where the budgets live), probed on all four shapes (files.ts, codemod/bin.ts and package.json run size-limit; apps/docs/guide/installation.md skips); the comment states the rule and the reason.

The failed job was re-run at 09:19Z: attempt 2 completed at 09:20:46Z with every job green (run 42000484240 SUCCESS; run dir ci-watch-cdc05f2-rerun.log), so the tree at cdc05f2 is proven end to end: Built-package tests with the 755 bins out of the tar, the four Test matrices with the SEC-57 and SEC-58 titles, Rust & WASM, Bundle Size under the 100 kB budget, Links.

**Security review of the 0a41f02 push, and W3-01f (d52d6f2; SEC-59).** The third review (CWE-426, MEDIUM) named the one case the nearest-work-tree rule gets wrong: with cwd inside a submodule checkout the nearest `.git` is the submodule's `.git` file (`gitdir: ../.git/modules/<name>`), so the boundary was the submodule and the superproject around it, cloned from the same untrusted source, stayed searchable (a planted git at <super>/bin on PATH ran for a dirty check from <super>/inner: red first). Ruling: trustBoundary climbs from a submodule to the superproject that holds the `.git/modules` directory its gitdir target lies under (superprojectOf takes the last `.git`/`modules` pair of the realpath; superprojectAbove reads the file, matches `/^gitdir:\s*(.+?)\s*$/m`, resolves against the submodule and realpaths; any read, match or realpath failure stops at the submodule), and again while that superproject is itself a submodule; a `.git` directory (an ordinary repository, a nested one included) stops the climb, which keeps the dotfiles-at-home exception; a linked worktree's `.git` file (`.git/worktrees`) stops it, since the main repository of a worktree is the user's own. The review's two other suggestions are declined: validating that `.git` holds a HEAD (git refuses to add any path component named .git, so a clone cannot carry a decoy `.git`; a dependency's install script that could plant one runs arbitrary code already), and dropping every node_modules/.bin PATH entry wherever it sits (the boundary already covers the clone's own node_modules, and the resolver's rule stays one sentence: PATH outside the migrated tree). Tests (SEC-59 titles in codemod-submodule.test.ts, with plantGit and scratchRepo helpers): inner and inner/deep bound to realpath(super); super > mid > leaf climbs to super; a planted git at super/bin with findDirtyFiles run from super/inner reads clean; a linked worktree and an ordinary nested repository are their own boundary. 3 red first of 74 with files.ts untouched; then the files, submodule and CLI suites 137 passed, the CI command 1229 passed and 1 skipped, both packages' tsc and tests exit 0, Biome clean, the dist project 57 passed after a rebuild, the dry-run unchanged, own files 97.92 kB of the 100 kB budget (sonnet-worker, 179 s); the orchestrator's gate on the merged tree: vitest 1286 passed and 1 skipped in 65 files, pnpm typecheck exit 0, Biome clean (run dir w301f-gate.log). The worker's one note stands: gitlinksOf still lists one submodule level, as its comment says (SEC-56, 1.6.0).

**Ruling 1, SEC-60: the first-publish job may not both run npm-resolved code and hold the signing credential.** The W4-05 verifier (important) found that round 1's first-publish-tarball held id-token: write and attestations: write while its smoke step npm-installed the packed codemod without a lockfile and executed the installed bin, so any transitive package could have attested arbitrary artifacts under this repository's identity. Ruling: split the job. first-publish-tarball keeps every step up to the upload with contents: read only and exposes state, tarball and sha256 outputs from a Hash step; first-publish-attest (needs it; state first; contents: read, id-token: write, attestations: write, artifact-metadata: write) downloads the artifact, refuses anything but exactly one tarball whose name and sha256 equal those outputs, unpacks it for syft, attests provenance and SBOM with the same pinned actions, uploads the SPDX file as first-publish-modern-cmdk-codemod-sbom, and runs no setup-node, pnpm, npm or repository script and no checkout (the actions/attest README mentions checkout only in its build examples; the anchore/sbom-action README never). Accepted deviations: artifact-metadata: write stays because the moved attest steps hold it today; the per-step ifs became the job if; the extra SBOM upload keeps the SPDX the owner used to get from one artifact. The header now names attest, first-publish-attest and publish as the only id-token: write jobs and says why the smoke job holds none; the version-job comment agrees with the scope gate (apply-release-plan 8.1.1 dist/index.mjs:180 skips workspace:*). The layout on disk after download-artifact, and attest and sbom-action without a checkout, are proven only by the owner's release run (W8-01); owner-gated-1.5.0.md section 1 now downloads both artifacts, checks the sha256 file, verifies both attestations and reads source.txt and the resolved tree.

**Ruling 2: the flow licence text and the noticeFiles key.** flow-estree@0.337.0 and flow-parser@0.337.0 (bundled through jscodeshift) ship README.md, package.json and dist only, no licence file; both declare MIT and the facebook/flow repository. The generator must neither skip them (MIT redistribution needs the notice) nor fetch at generation time (offline, deterministic). Ruling: pin the facebook/flow LICENSE (MIT, "Copyright (c) Meta Platforms, Inc. and affiliates."; last commit 741104e69c43057ebd32804dd6bcc1b5e97548ea of 2021-12-30; fetched 2026-11-05 through the GitHub API; 1088 bytes, LF, sha256 da6d3703ed11cbe42bd212c725957c98da23cbff1998c05fa4b3d976d1a58e93) as tests/package/notices/flow-0.337.0-LICENSE.txt, named by two pins (package, version, file, source blob URL, fetched, reason; all required) and rendered with one provenance line above the verbatim text. The worker put the pins under a new license-policy.json key, noticeFiles, because scripts/check-licenses.ts owns licenseFiles for hash-pinned files inside installed packages (khroma) and rejects any other shape: accepted, the key is documented in the script header and the policy. The orchestrator read the generated file outside its fences: 10 sections, each with name@version, Licence: and File: lines, the two flow sections carrying the provenance line, 0 CR bytes.

**Ruling 3: scaffold bytes and SEC-52.** The W4-01 verifier declined to judge whether the brief's byte-for-byte rule covers the create bin's scaffold output: the create cases check exit codes, stderr, file lists and package names, not the template bytes, which carry a version injected at build time. SEC-52 ends PARTIAL: the CLI driver, the create CLI and telemetry are covered; the remainder is SEC-02's frecency flush-on-dispose test (1.6.0). **Ruling 5: the W4-04b regression (DT-70) and the fix rounds c and d.** The wave-4 ledger judge (wf_f73d1758-505, an opus-verifier reading a6cf620 beside the running wave 5) found that W4-04b's killTree sends process.kill(-pid, 'SIGKILL') on POSIX and ignored ESRCH while the `next start` server spawned in consumer-smoke.ts's main was not detached: the group kill hit ESRCH, the error was swallowed and the server survived, holding its port and the script's event loop (a WSL probe: non-detached child, kill(-pid) gives ESRCH, child alive); before a6cf620 child.kill('SIGKILL') on the direct child had stopped it. Ruling: fix it in this wave, not defer it. W4-04c (a sonnet-worker, its diff read by the orchestrator; 631eeee) exports spawnServer (detached: !IS_WINDOWS, piped stdio) for the served app and gives killTree an ESRCH fallback to child.kill('SIGKILL') when the child is alive (exitCode and signalCode both null), success only when the child is already gone, rethrow for anything else; W4-04d mirrors the fallback in template-smoke.ts and exports killTree for its test. On win32 none of the new tests can go red (taskkill /T kills any tree), so the evidence is WSL Ubuntu-26.04 with node 26.10.0 through standalone scripts importing the exported functions: spawnServer plus killTree {alive: true} before the fix and {alive: false} after; the run() grandchild {timedOut: true, elapsed: 1507, alive: false}; the non-leader fallback {nonLeaderAlive: false}; the two smoke test files 43 passed on Windows. CI then showed the regression live and the fix (the CI paragraph above: the two Next consumer jobs at their 25-minute timeout on a6cf620, green again on c88d186). **CI.** Run 42013071098 on c88d186 completed success at the first attempt, 10:43:28Z to 10:46:37Z (3 min 9 s), 27 jobs: Templates 10:45:08Z to 10:45:36Z (28 s); Rust & WASM 55 s, the first run of the Binaryen cache key, which it necessarily missed; the three Consumer app jobs 49 to 55 s each where a6cf620's two Next jobs had run to their 25-minute timeout (G-38); the windows-2025 Node 28 Test lane green. **Ruling 1: the lychee self-exclusion trades coverage for determinism, and the comment says so.** Run 41998415732 had failed on a GitHub 504 for this repository's own issues URL after lychee.toml's six retries. W5-01's worker first skipped the hardening with the claim that an exclude in the action args would replace the toml's lists; the verifier refuted it from lychee 0.24.2 config/mod.rs:980 (the lists chain) and the fix round added `--exclude '^https://github\.com/ABCrimson/modern-cmdk(\.git)?([/#?]|$)'`. The verifier then failed the comment, which credited the checkout with proving the excluded links exist: true of the bare repository URL, false of blob/main/LICENSE (8 links), the benchmark JSON paths under blob/main and blob/5f1e01e, tree/main/packages/command-search-wasm, /discussions (a repository setting) and /security/advisories/new. Ruling: keep the exclusion, since retrying a GitHub 504 for longer does not converge; state the trade in the comment (W5-01c: "no job checks those links any more, the blob/ and tree/ paths into this repository included"); carry the gap forward as a deferred item, not a ledger row (the ledger holds defects of the shipped code and its tests; this is a known coverage gap of a hygiene job). Re-arm: a path-aware check of the blob/main links against the checkout, before the 1.5.0 PR (D63) or in 1.6.0's CI item.

**Ruling 3: the freshness sweep flagged actions/cache v6.1.0 as behind v5.1.0; the pin stands and the instrument was fixed.** `gh release list -R actions/cache` at 10:46Z: v6.1.0 marked Latest, published 2026-06-26T19:17:06Z; v5.1.0 published 2026-06-26T20:21:47Z, a maintenance release of the v5 line an hour later; the tag v6.1.0 resolves to 55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9, the pinned SHA. **Ruling 7: the lychee coverage gap of G-39 Ruling 1 closes in 1.5.0, as its re-arm clause allowed, and the check never writes to the checkout.** W8-05 adds scripts/check-repo-links.ts (`pnpm check:repo-links`, Node's native type stripping like check-doc-samples.ts): every https://github.com/ABCrimson/modern-cmdk/(blob|tree|raw)/<ref>/<path> link in the Markdown lychee scans plus the CHANGELOGs and .changeset/*.md is checked against the checkout (main and HEAD against the working tree; a commit, branch or tag ref through `git cat-file -e`, looked up under refs/remotes/origin and refs/tags before any fetch; `#readme` on a tree link needs a README.md; `#L` ranges within the file; heading fragments by GitHub's slug rule, ATX and setext, emphasis stripped), the routes GitHub serves from repository state are counted as unchecked and named (/actions, /commit, /discussions, /issues, /pull, /releases, /security on this repository), and the Links job runs it after lychee and setup-node, on a checkout with fetch-depth 0 because an abbreviated hash cannot be fetched by name; the comment at ci.yml:380-384 says what is and is not checked. **SEC-61 and SEC-62 (ledger-judge-150-sec61-62, wf_da3f9265-1c4: one opus-verifier, 106,649 tokens, 4 min 59 s, at e297590).** SEC-61 FIXED: every git argv at HEAD carries --end-of-options before a link-supplied argument (rev-parse --verify :282, fetch :291-298, cat-file -t, cat-file blob and ls-tree :349, :356, :366; rev-parse --is-shallow-repository takes nothing from a link); lookup rejects any ref that is not main, HEAD, hex or REF_NAME before any git call; the probes %2D%2Dupload-pack=touch%20pwned, %252D%252D, %2E%2E, release%2F1.5.0, -x, --output=pwned2 and x%0A--upload-pack=y were all dead with the reason, no GIT_TRACE file and no marker, while a real git call does create the trace file; the f1482d7 script fails both SEC-61 tests and the clone test. SEC-62 PARTIAL on two containment gaps and one output minor: the `..` check lived only in #inWorkingTree, so blob/<sha>/../secret.md under a --root subdirectory was read through git (`#L99 is outside the file's 3 lines`) and the header sentence was false; the check was lexical, so a committed symbolic link pointing outside the root was read (`#L999 is outside the file's 5 lines`); and the output lines printed a decoded ref, path or fragment raw, so %0A split one link across two lines and a line could start with `::warning::`, `::error::` or `::notice::` (a workflow command on a runner). The judge wrote the three corrected specs with red-first tests; W9-00 applied them (SEC-62's open clause; SEC-65 for the output escaping). Both rows are filed in this apply: SEC-61 FIXED at e297590, SEC-62 FIXED at 44ef752.

**D65 (orchestrator): the four discovery differences of the deprecated `modern-cmdk` bin are accepted under LD-05 as consequences of dropping globby 16, and the public record cites this ruling.** W1-02 (a302e98) replaced globby with the repository's own discovery (packages/modern-cmdk-codemod/src/files.ts, shared by both programs). tests/unit/codemod-files.test.ts pins four differences from 1.4 for both bins: a symlinked directory is not descended by `**` (globby 16 followed it); a symlinked file is not a file for discovery (globby 16 matched it); on win32 a pattern matches file names case-insensitively (globby 16 matched case-sensitively); a directory argument covers only the ten script and stylesheet extensions. LD-05 forbids a 1.x behaviour change that restores no documented contract; these four restore none, but each is a safety or portability correction of a path no 1.4 document promised (1.4's README documented the excludes and the glob form, not symlink traversal or case rules), each is pinned by a test, and the alternative (re-implementing globby 16's symlink-following and case-sensitive matching for the deprecated bin alone) would ship a second discovery program for a bin that leaves in 2.0. Ruling: accepted; the changeset's 'Differences from 1.4 under the bin' bullet cites D65 (W9-06), the six docs sites say the bin keeps "1.4's excludes" and name the four differences (DG-135, W9-07). Status: LOCKED.

**D66 (orchestrator): `<Command.Item onSelect>` stays `() => void` in 1.x; the value argument is a 2.0 change (ARCH-62).** e4fc79b (W1-05, the second half of ARCH-11) changed the React prop to `(value: string) => void`. The 1.4 docs documented `() => void`; a 1.4 handler whose first parameter does not accept a string (`toggle(force?: boolean)`) fails tsc against 1.5 and receives a string at run time (the final review's finding 20; its `onSelect={setOpen}` example was wrong: a boolean `Dispatch<SetStateAction<boolean>>` fails tsc against `() => void` too, TS2322 "Target signature provides too few arguments", so it never type-checked in 1.4 either, as the wave-9 ledger judge wf_9ec6e870-b25 proved with a probe). LD-05 (no breaking change in a 1.x minor unless it restores a documented contract) is not met: cmdk's `onSelect(value)` is cmdk's contract, not modern-cmdk's. Option A, chosen: revert in the release (W9-08: item.tsx and use-register.ts back to `() => void`, the JSDoc saying the value arrives in 2.0; tests/unit/item-onselect-value.test.tsx re-pinned to zero arguments on click, Enter and shortcut and to a `(force?: boolean) => void` handler type-checking; 0336a5f's message also claims a boolean state setter type-checks as onSelect, which is false and corrected here; the two API pages back to `() => void`; the changeset bullet and the guide section removed). Option B (keep the argument and call it a bug fix) was rejected: no 1.x document ever promised it. ARCH-11's fix text is amended to say so; ARCH-62 (MODERNIZE, 2.0.0) carries the change forward in the roadmap's section 4 list. The core `CommandItem` type was never changed. Status: LOCKED.

**D67 (orchestrator): a bounded, announced retry of an infrastructure install is not an LD-04 test retry.** LD-04 forbids retrying tests. ci.yml's `pnpm playwright install --with-deps chromium` is an apt-backed browser install whose only recurring failure is the runner image's apt lists (run 42023125730, G-40 Ruling 5: one of three identical jobs failed with apt exit 100 after fetching only packages.microsoft.com; the re-run's step took 427 s against 15-18 s). W9-03 gives the three jobs a three-attempt loop that prints a `::warning::` annotation on every failed attempt, runs `apt-get update || true` and waits 15 s between attempts, and exits 1 after the third, so a flake is visible in the run's annotations and a real failure still fails. Ruling: this is infrastructure, not a test; no test, gate or check in the repository retries; any future retry of this kind must announce every attempt the same way. Status: LOCKED.

**D74 (orchestrator): a stacked branch merges main against its fork point.** After #60's rebase merge, PR #61 (release/1.5.0 -> main) reported `mergeable_state: dirty` and `rebaseable: false`: release/1.5.0 was cut from release/1.4.0 at b77f133, main holds a rebase-merged copy of that lineage with new SHAs, so git's computed merge base predates it and `git merge-tree` reports a conflict on every 1.5.0 hunk that touches a line the 1.4.0 lineage also changed (40 files in the preview; `git cherry origin/main release/1.5.0`: 61 of the branch's 177 commits patch-equivalent to main, 116 new). The remedy under D40 (merge, never rebase a pushed branch): `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base=b77f133 origin/main release/1.5.0` (git 2.56.0) merges against the true fork point and is clean; `git commit-tree <tree> -p release/1.5.0 -p origin/main` makes the regular two-parent merge commit a2c6b41 (merge-main-into-150.sh in the scratchpad, merge-main-into-150.log in the run dir). What main brought is exactly its two release commits: the consumed changesets only-green-honest-gates.md and package-integrity-public-record.md deleted, the 1.3.0 and 1.4.0 (1.3.1) CHANGELOG sections, package versions 1.4.0 and 1.3.1 (6 files, 152 insertions, 77 deletions). `pnpm changeset status --verbose` then plans exactly modern-cmdk-codemod 0.0.0 -> 1.0.0 (major, modern-cmdk-codemod-1-0-0.md), create-modern-cmdk 1.3.1 -> 1.4.0 (minor, create-modern-cmdk-1-5-0.md) and modern-cmdk 1.4.0 -> 1.5.0 (minor, modern-cmdk-1-5-0.md). Rule going forward: when a release branch stacks on a parent that reaches main by rebase, main is merged into it against the recorded fork point (the merge commit's message names it); before `gh pr merge --rebase`, the API's `rebaseable` flag is read; if GitHub cannot rebase the duplicate-bearing history, the fallback is a fresh branch `git rebase --onto main <fork point>` carrying only the new commits, opened as a new PR with the old one closed by a comment that names it, never a squash (D59's per-commit citations need the commits). **SEC-70 (commit cddf64b; ledger e6e66d9).** scripts/consumer-smoke.ts exports `resolvePnpmEntry(env, nodePath)` (the file npm_execpath names plus leading arguments, undefined outside pnpm; the basename must match /^pnpm(?:[-.].*)?$/i, so npm's npm-cli.js is rejected), `spawnTarget(command, args, env)` (maps 'pnpm' through the resolver, every other command unchanged) and `pnpmUsage(script, pnpmScript)`; template-smoke.ts imports them (it already imported from consumer-smoke.ts; there is no scripts/lib). An executable entry is spawned directly; a .cjs, .mjs or .js entry runs through process.execPath with the entry first; outside pnpm both scripts print the usage line and exit 2. Timeouts, the taskkill tree-kill, output capture and step logging are unchanged. The old comment "The arguments are constants of this file, never user input" is replaced by the mechanism; the JSDoc names the CodeQL rule and cites the installed Node's own docs, https://nodejs.org/download/nightly/v28.0.0-nightly20261107a43bfb5bb2/docs/api/child_process.html "Spawning .bat and .cmd files on Windows", read 2026-11-07. Tests: 11 new cases red on the unchanged scripts (template-smoke.test.ts failed to load for the missing import), 61 green across template-smoke, consumer-smoke and ci-workflow; a source-text assertion keeps `cmd.exe` and `"/d", "/c"` out of both scripts. End to end on this host: `pnpm build` 0; `pnpm check:package --pack-dir …` 0 (create-modern-cmdk-1.3.1.tgz, modern-cmdk-1.4.0.tgz, modern-cmdk-codemod-0.0.0.tgz); `pnpm test:templates --pack-dir … --template react-basic` 0 (PASS); `pnpm test:consumers` 0 (next-turbopack, next-webpack, vite: 1 core copy, ssr true, dialog true, hydration 0); `node scripts/template-smoke.ts` and `node scripts/consumer-smoke.ts` with npm_execpath unset exit 2 with the usage line; through an npm-installed pnpm 12.18.0 (`node …/pnpm/bin/pnpm.mjs run test:templates …`) exit 0: that package ships bin/pnpm.mjs, not pnpm.cjs, and pnpm.mjs re-execs @pnpm/exe's pnpm.exe and sets npm_execpath to the .exe, so the direct branch ran; a forced `npm_execpath=…/pnpm.mjs node scripts/template-smoke.ts …` exit 0 exercised the JavaScript-entry branch. The worker's claim that pnpm/action-setup installs the standalone executable so CI takes the executable branch was wrong on two counts, and the fix was never run on a runner: the first two CI runs that carried it, 42440217112 (e6e66d9) and 42441310879 (4e5b933), failed Consumer app (next-turbopack, next-webpack, vite), Templates and CI Summary with `Process completed with exit code 2` before the first smoke step, because ci.yml ran the scripts as `node scripts/consumer-smoke.ts …` and `node scripts/template-smoke.ts …`: bare node, no npm_execpath, the usage path; and the runner's PNPM_HOME (/home/runner/setup-pnpm/node_modules/.bin) shows action-setup installs the npm package, not @pnpm/exe. **SEC-72 (BUG, high, 1.6.0; fix 658007c, ledger 296dde0):** both steps run `pnpm run test:consumers --app ${{ matrix.variant }} --report consumers-${{ matrix.variant }}.json` and `pnpm run test:templates --pack-dir "$RUNNER_TEMP/pack" --template all` with a comment above each saying why (pnpm 12 forwards the flags after the script name: `pnpm run test:consumers --app bogus` reaches the parser on this host and names bogus; bare node prints the usage line and exits 2); tests/unit/ci-workflow.test.ts pins both step lines and forbids a bare `node scripts/…-smoke.ts` step anywhere in ci.yml (red 2 of 3 on the old workflow, 62 green across ci-workflow, consumer-smoke and template-smoke after); the resolvePnpmEntry comment states what action-setup installs (the npm package whose bin/pnpm.mjs re-execs the native pnpm of its platform package when present, so either branch may run on the runner); both usage lines read `usage: pnpm test:consumers …` and `usage: pnpm test:templates …` instead of naming the invocation the scripts refuse. Rule going forward (joins the RDG-125 rule): a change to a CI step's command or to what a CI-run script requires of its environment is verified by a CI run on the branch before its row is marked FIXED; "verified on this host" is not that. `pnpm test` on the fix: 1637 passed, 3 skipped. Rebased onto 6171765 without conflict and fast-forwarded onto release/1.6.0. The CodeQL alerts #17 and #10 close when the branch's PR runs CodeQL (pending). One footnote: `pnpm run <script> -- --flag` under pnpm 12 forwards the `--` literally, so the smoke scripts see `-- --pack-dir`; pass flags without the separator.

**D73, first half (commit a8c550e, cherry-picked from 1.6/d73 d1dc5a1).** release.yml loses the `backfill` job (its `if`, `needs: ci-gate`, checkout and "Create the missing tags and releases" step, and the comment block), the whole `workflow_dispatch:` trigger with `inputs.task` (backfill was the only option, so the input was pointless; `on:` is `push: branches: [main]` alone), `select-mode`'s `if: github.event_name == 'push'`, and the dispatch lines of the header comment; the concurrency group becomes `release-${{ github.ref }}` (was `release-${{ github.event_name }}-${{ github.ref }}`, unique per ref either way); the comment above first-publish-tarball reads "Temporary:" instead of "Temporary, like backfill:". The jobs first-publish-tarball, first-publish-smoke and first-publish-attest are untouched (the second half, after modern-cmdk-codemod 1.0.0 is on npm with its trusted publisher) and every surviving `uses:` is byte-identical. tests/unit/release-workflow.test.ts: the D62 backfill suite is replaced by "release.yml has no backfill job and no dispatch trigger (D73)" with four tests (the job list is exactly ci-gate, select-mode, version, first-publish-tarball, first-publish-smoke, first-publish-attest, pack, attest, publish and `backfill` appears nowhere; no workflow_dispatch, inputs., task: or github.event_name; every `needs` and job-level `if` of the surviving graph; the header names no dispatch run): red 4 of 21 on the old file, green 23 of 23 with ci-workflow. CONTRIBUTING.md's "backfill task" paragraph under "### Release tags" is deleted and the tree line reads "release.yml  Wait for CI, then changeset version / publish". Parsed with the installed yaml 2.9.1: `on = {"push":{"branches":["main"]}}`, nine jobs. Not verified: the rewritten concurrency group on GitHub (the workflow has not run from the branch). Follow-up for DOCS: CONTRIBUTING.md still says every tag from modern-cmdk@1.2.3 on is created by the release workflow, never by hand, while the codemod's 1.0.0 tag and release will be created by the orchestrator through `gh release create` (D68): reword when that release exists.

**TUB-77 (ledger 7ff5c63, OPEN, TEST, medium, 1.6.0, cluster CORE/tests).** tests/unit/codemod-submodule.test.ts builds a scratch origin, superproject, submodule, nested submodule and worktree with real git subprocesses in mkdtemp directories for every test; on 2026-11-08, with six agent worktrees installing and testing on this host, "SEC-59: a submodule of a submodule climbs to the outermost superproject" timed out at 30 s in the pre-push run (then `EBUSY: resource busy or locked, rmdir C:\Users\alber\.claude-tmp\modern-cmdk-sub-onvkPu\mid`), the same test failed the same way in the replay's full run at c82bcee, and the file alone took 209 s with SEC-59 timing out again and SEC-55's hook timing out; 69 modern-cmdk-sub-* directories were left under the temp directory, so the teardown has leaked across runs. The tests pass on CI's isolated runner and on an idle host; the row's plan: fixtures once per file in beforeAll, teardown with fs.rm maxRetries inside try/finally, one measured beforeAll timeout, a no-leak assertion; no raised per-test timeout (LD-04). The pre-push retry was an announced infrastructure retry (D67), not a test retry.

**The 1.6.0 brief (R/plan-1.6.0-context.md, final; rows in R/plan-1.6.0-rows.md: 157 rows with release 1.6.0 at 2387816, all placed in sub-items A to O, none unplaced; 160 now with SEC-70, SEC-71, TUB-77).** The orchestrator's rulings, each flagged in the owner report and open to the owner's reversal before 1.6.0 ships:
- **D77 (R1, landing).** release/1.6.0 takes linear commits (cherry-pick or rebase, never a merge commit) in the order: sweep, SEC-70, SEARCH, CORE, BENCH, CODEMOD, the orchestrator's ledger and matrix commits, DOCS, the changeset(s), the RC gate run; every cluster branches from the sha the dispatch names and is rebased by the orchestrator at landing; D73's second half follows the codemod's publish.
- **D78 (R2, disabled contract; D1 path (a), adopted default).** `disabledItems: 'hide' | 'show'`, default 'show' through 2.x; under 'show' disabled items are listed and rendered with aria-disabled and data-disabled in every branch (empty query, `filter: false`, a query), never first active, skipped by navigate() and ITEM_ACTIVATE, ITEM_SELECT a no-op; under 'hide' dropped from filteredIds everywhere. A labelled bug fix restoring apps/docs/guide/accessibility.md:41-50 and the CommandItem contract (core/types.ts:52-53 reworded, CE-32); the exit-gate matrix in tests/unit/disabled-matrix.test.ts plus React assertions; the changeset label fixed verbatim in the brief.
- **D79 (R3, core size budget, D19).** Measure, trim, then stop: every CORE item reports `pnpm size` before and after; 6500 B core, 5600 react, 1500 react/dialog hold; if every item in cannot fit, the cluster stops at the measured number and the orchestrator decides between a `modern-cmdk/frecency` subpath (additive, the core re-export kept through 1.x) and a raised limit (owner decision). First measurement: the batching item alone (1.6/machine ccbdda4) takes core from 6399 to 6436 B gzip (64 B headroom), react and dialog unchanged.
- **D80 (R4, floor guard).** Biome `useBaseline` is CSS-only, so the JS floor guard is tests/unit/browser-floor.test.ts over tests/unit/browser-floor.json (web-features ids the core and react entries rely on, each with a dist identifier pattern; a deny-list of newer APIs; the per-browser floor computed from web-features' support data and asserted equal to the published numbers; every listed feature Baseline; a parameterised negative "seeded newer API" case), `web-features` as a root devDependency at its newest build (Apache-2.0), `lint/suspicious/useBaseline` enabled for styles.css; the roadmap's "Safari 18.4 / Chrome 122 / Firefox 131" is the expected result and the docs line is written by DOCS from the test's output; roadmap line 265 corrected by DOCS.
- **D81 (R5, browser bench).** benchmarks/browser/ Playwright harness on the installed Chromium against a playground /bench route at N in {1000, 10000}: mount (first REGISTER_ITEM to the first state emit holding all N), filter (keystroke to emit), keystroke-to-paint; median of 7 after 1 warm-up; React commit recorded, not gated; results JSON committed at benchmarks/browser/results/<version>-<sha>.json (D27); a `browser-bench` CI job gating the 10K mount median at 50 ms, joining the CI Summary's needs; the Node mount series as benchmarks/mount.bench.ts with no absolute assertion; the gate is expected red on the BENCH branch and green on release/1.6.0 after CORE lands.
- **D82 (R6, frecency).** Trailing 1000 ms debounced save (a documented constant, no option); flush on visibilitychange-hidden and pagehide (idempotent); the machine gains `Symbol.asyncDispose` awaiting the flush while `Symbol.dispose` stays synchronous; the load is a handled promise that refilters on hydration and reports a rejection through the machine's error path; merge on load sums frequency and takes the max lastUsed; row validation drops malformed records with a counted warning; the IDB payload is versioned, the reader accepting v1 (bare map) and v2 (`{ version: 2, records }`), the writer staying v1 (1.11 writes v2); SEC-35 fixed by an upgrade creating the missing store; P07 (the machine disposes a passed-in storage) unchanged in 1.x, documented, moved to 2.0.0; the 10K-record fixture is a seeded generator (tests/fixtures/frecency/generate.ts), not committed data.
- **D83 (R7).** `Symbol.dispose ??= Symbol.for('Symbol.dispose')` and the asyncDispose twin as the first statements of core/index.ts, cited; the computed-key sites unchanged.
- **D84 (R8, scope lines).** UPDATE_ITEM: 1.6.0 adds the core event (in-place upsert, position kept, shortcut re-registered) and switches use-register.ts to it for in-place changes of value, disabled, shortcut and keywords; 1.7.0 keeps latest-ref options and item diffing. P26 is out unless SEARCH reproduces it with a measurement; ARCH-44, DAE-76, SPEC-32, CE-34 are in. Ledger housekeeping (orchestrator): F16 to release 2.0.0; SEC-52 to 1.6.0 with `remaining` SEC-02; DA-11, DA-12, SEC-56 re-verified by CODEMOD against packages/modern-cmdk-codemod/src/transforms; the MOVED rows re-read by their cluster; RDG-38, DT-11, RDG-34 re-verified at their new locations. `shortcutAction: 'select' | 'direct'`, default 'direct' through 1.x (no contract to restore; 2.0 flips it); conflicts tracked in getConflicts(), the later binding wins; ITEMS_LOADED registers shortcuts; score <= 0 excluded (ARCH-14, restores architecture/overview.md:126, label fixed in the brief); one refilter and one emit per batch, counted by tests; group priority orders groupedIds (TUB-06, core/types.ts:62); the EventMap constraint (TUB-05).
- **D85 (R9).** Chromium only for the bench and the e2e gate (Firefox and WebKit at 1.8.0, TEB-81).
- **D86 (R10).** DOCS runs last on release/1.6.0 and owns apps/docs/**, READMEs, CONTRIBUTING.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, roadmap line 265, core comments (K), test titles (L), vanilla examples (M), and the changeset(s) (the bug-matrix extension moved to the orchestrator under D88), writing the floor numbers and the frecency constants from the code (LD-03).
Clusters and ownership are in the brief (SEARCH; CORE/machine and CORE/frecency in parallel worktrees, frecency's machine.ts hunks in one final commit the orchestrator rebases onto machine's result; CORE/tests after both land; BENCH; CODEMOD; DOCS last). - **D91 (CE-113 held to 1.7.0).** `if (disposed) return;` in send() turns two pinned behaviours red: tests/unit/machine.test.ts:569 reuses a machine after dispose on purpose and tests/unit/command.test.tsx:255 relies on the adapter's StrictMode remount keeping the machine captured at render, which the first unmount disposed. Reuse after dispose is a deliberate 1.x behaviour until the adapter's StrictMode handling changes (CE-78, RA-03, 1.7.0); the guard lands with them. dispose() cancels the pending scheduler batch (scheduler[Symbol.dispose]()), so only a handler that disposes mid-batch lets the batch's remaining events through. Patch (+10 B core) kept at R/ce113-guard-and-t11.patch.
- **D92 (highlight ranges on a keyword win).** `SearchResult.matches` always indexes the original `item.value` (P20 maps the lowered offsets back through the one UTF-16-length-changing character, U+0130, every code point checked); when a keyword outscores the value, `matches` keeps the value's own ranges if the value matched and is empty otherwise (a keyword has no position in the label). The row's suggested optional field marker was not adopted; the JSDoc, apps/docs/guide/basic-usage.md:89 and the changeset state the rule.
- **D93 (the bench:compare noise rule; TEB-100).** compare-baseline.ts reports the median of five runs' p50 (p99 and rme likewise) instead of the mean of three; the CI step "Compare against baseline" re-runs the comparison once on exit 2 and is red only when both runs fail, the first failure surfaced as a `::warning`; exit 1 keeps its warning path; the thresholds (5 % warn, 15 % fail) and the 0.01 ms floor are unchanged; benchmarks/baseline.json is re-recorded from the benchmark-results artifact of the first green run after each Node pin bump, by the orchestrator, never by a worker. CONTRIBUTING states the rule. Flagged to the owner as an adopted default.
- **D83 amended (where the disposal-symbol shims live).** D83 (G-47) placed `Symbol.dispose ??= Symbol.for('Symbol.dispose')` and the asyncDispose twin as the first statements of core/index.ts. Since the CORE/frecency landing they live in core/frecency/idb-storage.ts:26-27: core/index.ts is re-exports only, a side-effect-only shim module would be dropped by the build (package.json `sideEffects` lists only styles.css), and idb-storage.ts is the first module core/index.ts evaluates that defines a class (its own import, utils/string-wellformed.ts, defines none), so the shims run before any class with a computed disposal key; './frecency/idb-storage.js' stays the first import of core/index.ts, and the file's header says all of this. tests/unit/browser-floor.test.ts (R7, 1.6/bench) proves on dist/core/index.mjs that the shims precede the first computed disposal key, that no top-level module load precedes or follows them, and that only inert statements (imports excluded, destructuring excluded) precede them. The frecency landing recorded the move in the file and in this row's frecency paragraph but DECISIONS.md:1121 kept the index.ts wording; this entry supersedes that wording, marked as such. machine.ts:7 cites 'D83 as amended by G-48'.

**TEB-102 (TEST, low, FIXED 8362840): the first push was rejected by the pre-push unit gate.** Three R4 tests red: 'R4: the allowed property %s is used by styles.css and reported once its allow entry is removed' for scroll-timeline, animation-timeline and animation-range. 1.6/bench branched at ff2745c, before the CSS landing, and added those three Biome useBaseline allowProperties entries (5c08a99); RA-77 (575be27) then wrapped the properties in `@supports (animation-timeline: scroll())`, and Biome 2.6.6's useBaseline does not report a property inside a matching @supports guard, so on the merged tree the entries were dead and removing them changed nothing (`Checked 1 file … No fixes applied`). The entries are removed; the allow list keeps clip and user-select, both still reported when removed (73 passed). `git merge-tree` had called the branches clean: the coupling is semantic. Rule going forward: a branch whose tests pin another cluster's file is re-run on the merged tree before the push (the pre-push gate is the backstop that fired, as D88 intends).

**CI 42455685144 on 8362840 (pushed 06:39Z after the pre-push gate: 2132 passed, size exit 0).** 27 of 28 jobs green; the run red on one.
- **Browser bench (Chromium, 10K mount gate): success, its first GitHub run** (06:41:12–06:42:15Z; ubuntu-26.04 image 20261101.186.1, AMD EPYC 7763 at 4 cores, Node v28.0.0-nightly20261107a43bfb5bb2, Chromium 158.0.8127.18 new headless): N=10000 mount median 47.80 ms (min 40.70, max 59.70; samples 47.8, 43.7, 40.7, 59.7, 58.9, 51.0, 44.4), mount_commit 145.30 ms; N=1000 mount 27.40 ms; 18.80 ms for the 10K mount on the development host. Artifact browser-bench-results saved under the run directory (artifacts/42455685144/).
- **Benchmarks: success under D93.** Median of five: search:ts-scorer-10k 3.462 → 3.779 ms (+9.2 %), stateUpdate:searchChange 0.368 → 0.399 ms (+8.6 %): WARN (exit 1 → the 5 % warning annotation), no re-run needed. The offset against a baseline recorded under Node nightly 20261103 stays until the re-record (below).
- **React react-floor: failure (TUB-81, TEST, medium).** tests/unit/memory-leak.test.ts:133 'MEM-3: 10K unsubscribed listeners are released while the machine stays alive, and never fire': `expected [ WeakRef{} ] to have a length of +0 but got 1` after one `collectGarbage()`; the six Test matrix jobs and react-stable passed the same test, as did the pre-push gate. One survivor of ten thousand is GC nondeterminism, not a leak. Fix: 1.6/mem (cf19ea0, 07ccd89) landed as 9162ebf and 60a9f96, below.
- The run reads failure, so TEB-100 and TEB-101 close on the next fully green run, not this one (SEC-72 read literally); the jobs' results above are the evidence they will cite.

- **D94 (the browser-bench threshold is a measured tripwire; TEB-101).** The roadmap's 1.6.0 exit gate "10K mount <= 50 ms on the CI image per the bench" is met by the recorded median, 47.80 ms. Three of the seven samples were above 50 ms, so a 50 ms threshold on a 4-core runner is a coin flip over time. The job's threshold is set from the runner's measurement like D90 sets the size rows: 1.5x the measured median rounded up to 5 ms = 75 ms, re-measured at the RC and at each runner-image change, never raised silently; the recorded CI median is what the docs report (the CI record benchmarks/browser/results/1.4.0-8362840.json replaces the development-host stand-in 1.4.0-f5fcebb.json). Landed as d9e5e6c (1.6/gates 100727b): `MOUNT_GATE_THRESHOLD_MS` 50 → 75 in benchmarks/browser/stats.ts with the rule and the measurement in its comment; the run.ts header, the ci.yml browser-bench comment and the R5 pins of tests/unit/bench-compare-rules.test.ts follow (the constant mutated back to 50 turns two R5 tests red); the CI record added and the stand-in deleted (the record is deep-equal to the artifact). 0a1f0ac rewrites the two hang-bound comments (ci.yml timeout-minutes, run.ts TIMEOUT_MS) to the batched harness's measured walls (10K runs 1764 to 1974 ms, 1K 493 to 528 ms, the harness step about 21 s, the job 62 s); the bound values stay (about 60x the slowest run). The roadmap's three 10K-mount lines record the measurement and the tripwire (78f7753, after the code).
- **D93's instrument.** benchmarks/standalone/record-baseline.ts (`pnpm bench:record <results.json> --source <run>`) re-records benchmarks/baseline.json from a benchmark-results artifact, carrying target_ms and floor_ms over and rewriting the note; landed as 7b8914c, d846377 and 26f5c4c (1.6/gates 8e72a3b, cbf9c0f, 85649da). The first verifier returned FIX FIRST on 8e72a3b: `source` held a run id, which D27 forbids (the source names the committed benchmarks/history file); `version` carried the stale "1.2.3" over (the recorded commit is 1.4.0); the note always said five runs; `--target k=Infinity` passed; a misattributed noise sentence. d846377: the positional argument is the committed `benchmarks/history/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<sha7>.json` (it must sit directly under benchmarks/history/ and its name must match its own `date` (UTC day) and `commit`), `version` comes from `git show <commit>:packages/modern-cmdk/package.json` (a `--repo` flag makes the test hermetic with a throwaway repo, identity set with -c, GIT_* scrubbed), `runs` must be 5, finite positive targets, the note reworded; benchmarks/history/2026-11-08-8362840.json is the byte-identical copy of the run's artifact. Re-verified PASS (every negative path exits 2 and writes nothing; the version and runs mutants go red; UTC day holds under TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati); its four minor findings are 26f5c4c: `commit` must be a 40-hex sha before it reaches git, `--target` values must be decimal, the UTC day has a test (TZ America/Chicago and Pacific/Auckland; the local-day mutant goes red), and tests/tsconfig.json type-checks the recorder (31 tests). The re-record itself happens from the first fully green run's artifact: from the first fully green run's benchmark-results artifact, committed as benchmarks/history/<date>-<sha7>.json and recorded with `pnpm bench:record` (next row). The re-record lands as its own commit after that run: a baseline recorded from run N is compared by run N+1..

**TUB-81 fix (9162ebf, 60a9f96).** `collectGarbage(settled)` in tests/unit/memory-leak.test.ts runs up to 20 major collections, each `await gc({ type: 'major', execution: 'async', flavor: 'last-resort' })` followed by a `setImmediate` turn, and returns as soon as the caller's predicate holds; every call site passes the predicate it then asserts (`allCollected(refs)` for the two WeakRef-array tests, `listenerRef.deref() === undefined` for the dispose test) and the strict-zero assertions are unchanged. Verifier (PASS, then two minor fixes in 60a9f96: the predicate is required and the dead no-predicate path is gone; the comment states the probed reason): on Node 28.0.0-nightly20261107 (V8 15.6.75.33-node.11) `type: 'major'` is a Mark-Compact, `flavor: 'last-resort'` two reduce rounds, `execution: 'async'` returns a promise that resolves after the collection, which runs as its own task after the caller's microtask checkpoint, so the predicate's own `deref()` cannot pin a target (a synchronous gc() would); invalid option values silently fall back to a Scavenge, which @types/node's typing of gc() guards against; a kept listener still turns MEM-3 red ('got 1'; all 10000 kept: 'got 10000'); 20 passes under load take about 156 ms against the 30 s timeout. The flake did not reproduce locally (100 runs idle and under load, 0 failures); this row's CI run is the evidence. Incident recorded: the worker's first load script ended with `taskkill /F /IM node.exe` and ran once (about 07:00Z), which kills every node process on the host, the ledger dashboard among them; every brief since says PID-only kills, and the memory file no-image-name-process-kills.md keeps the rule.

- **D95 (frecency storage ownership follows creation; CE-115).** A storage the caller passes in `frecency.storage` (core option or React prop) is the caller's to dispose; the engine and the machine dispose only a storage they created (today the default MemoryFrecencyStorage). Why: under React StrictMode the development double-mount runs the effect cleanup, which disposed the machine, the engine and with it the caller's storage, so the re-mounted machine saved nothing (`<Command frecency={{ enabled: true, storage: new IdbFrecencyStorage() }}>` persisted 0 records; the DOCS cluster had documented a view-object workaround). The previous contract ("the machine owns the storage it is given", DG-61's wording on 1.6/docs) is superseded; the frecency pages, the api page and the changeset say the new rule in the docs follow-up. 1.6/frecency-race (sonnet-worker; 6868bfb, 18f8093, e65a15c, then the verifier's round cb897f5, 241116c) landed as d1bd161, 5676bb6, 4d0c90d, 8c683cf and 940012b: CE-114 (a never-rejecting `#loading` every flush awaits; three tests; the unchained mutant red), CE-115 (`#ownsStorage`, `#disposeStorage`; the FrecencyOptions.storage JSDoc; tests at the engine, the machine and a StrictMode render; the forced-dispose mutant red), CE-122 (the machine's JSDoc example), the three comment corrections and D96 (`#loadFailed`; the rejecting-load test asserts flush() and asyncDispose() settle with no save; the flag mutant red). Verifier 1 FIX FIRST (two false test comments, the hook comment, the storage-first `using` order in the docs, the D96 question), verifier 2 PASS: 2020 unit tests, sizes core 7.38 kB / react 5.61 kB / core+react 10.82 kB against 7.4 / 5.7 / 10.85 (D90), the machine warns on a failed load (machine.ts:118-121), not the engine; `#loadFailed` never resets, which the "call load() once" JSDoc covers.
- **Code defects found by the DOCS cluster's verifiers, filed as CE-114 to CE-122.** CE-114 (BUG, high, 1.6.0): a save before load() settled overwrote the stored history (load was not chained into #pending; probed with a delayed-load storage). CE-115 (BUG, high, 1.6.0): the StrictMode disposal above. CE-116 (BUG, high, 1.7.0): the WASM engine's index() replaces the index instead of adding (command-search-wasm is private; filed for the release that builds it in CI). CE-117 (BUG, medium, 1.7.0): onOpenChange echoes on every OPEN/CLOSE including mount and CLOSE-while-closed. CE-118 (BUG, low, 1.7.0): Escape sends CLOSE twice in a dialog. CE-119 (BUG, low, 1.7.0): the virtualized list scrolls the active row to the top on every activation, hover included. CE-120 (TEST_GAP, low, 1.7.0): AsyncItems may register each loaded item twice (unverified). CE-121 (DEAD, low, 1.7.0): registry.differenceFrom has no caller in the shipped source. CE-122 (STALE, low, 1.6.0): the machine's JSDoc example used a top-level `using`. CE-123 (PERF, medium, 1.7.0; the docs verifier): when filteredCount crosses the virtualization threshold list.tsx swaps wrapper trees and React remounts every Command.Item, so the 10K mount sends 20000 events (RA-78's number, now explained). CE-124 (BUG, medium, 1.7.0; the frecency verifier): under StrictMode the subtree keeps the disposed machine until the root re-renders, so a keystroke before that re-render is dropped (development only; the hook comment that claimed a fresh machine is corrected in this push). CE-114, CE-115 and CE-122 are fixed in this row's push on 1.6/frecency-race; the rest are 1.7.0 rows.
- **D96 (a rejected frecency load turns persistence off for that engine).** With CE-114 a save waits for the load; when the load rejects, writing the engine's records would replace a history it could not read, so the engine writes nothing for the rest of its life and the machine's console.warn (CE-14, SEC-27) is the signal. A load that never settles holds every save and `await using` with it; that is the documented contract (the verifier's probe P3), not a bug.

### G-53 · 2026-11-08 · The C1 LIFECYCLE design review: NO-GO on the seeded-successor mechanism; D102 (the machine suspends and resumes across StrictMode and Activity), D103 (the machine disposes only a search engine it created); the measured headroom at b145c15; C1 dispatched

**What happened.** Before C1 dispatched, an opus-verifier reviewed the plan's lifecycle design read-only against the installed React (react and react-dom 0.0.0-experimental-5b610964-20261106, @types/react 19.3.0, radix-ui 1.8.2-rc, Vitest 5.1.0, happy-dom 21.0.4) with seven probes (R/c1-design-review.md holds the report verbatim). The plan's mechanism (the machine in `useState(factory)`, a `queueMicrotask` dispose cancelled by a same-task setup, a successor machine seeded from the last snapshot after an Activity hide) fails on the code: (F1) StrictMode calls a `useState` initializer twice and discards one result (react-dom-client.development.js:8881-8893; react.dev/reference/react/useState), and `createCommandMachine` has side effects at construction (the document keydown listener in KeyboardShortcutRegistry's constructor, keyboard/index.ts:120-123; the pagehide and visibilitychange listeners, frecency/index.ts:96-102; `storage.load()`, machine.ts:111; the leak sentinel, search/index.ts:52-56), so the discarded twin keeps a live keyboard listener and one ctrl+k fired an item's onSelect twice in the probe; the lazy null-guarded `useRef` initializer runs once and is the idiom react.dev sanctions. (F2) machine.ts:435 disposes the search engine unconditionally, a caller's included, so a successor built with the same `search` throws on its first REGISTER_ITEM. (F3) the constructor seeds only `open` (types.ts:194,247); seeding search by a send empties the controlled input for a frame (input.tsx:43-52) and a seeded activeId is dropped by the construction refilter (machine.ts:149,208-211). Items 2 to 5 of the design (latest-ref options, open state, ARCH-29, RA-36) pass with corrections: dedupe only onSearchChange, onActiveChange and onOpenChange with hook-scoped memory initialised from the machine's state, never onSelect, and no trampoline forwarding in controlled mode (F4); Radix's DismissableLayer owns Escape in the dialog and the document handler skips a defaultPrevented Escape, with `open = controlledOpen ?? state.open` computed once for Dialog.Root, both data-state attributes and the key-listener effect (F5); `shortcutAction` is read at bind time only (machine.ts:235,240) and stays init-only with a development warning, and a `disabledItems` change must refilter (F6); the lifecycle logic runs in a layout effect (F8). Confirmed: StrictMode's effect replay is synchronous (react-dom-client.development.js:22087-22102); Activity cleans up layout and passive effects and keeps state, and re-creates them on show (react.dev/reference/react/Activity); the server dispatcher's useLayoutEffect is a no-op (react-dom-server.node.development.js:11376); the machine's state identity is a valid snapshot cache key (machine.ts:153,381-383).

**D102 (orchestrator; resolves the plan's question 5).** The lifecycle primitive is a core `suspend()` / `resume()` pair on CommandMachine, public and documented, kept through 2.0. `suspend()` detaches every global hold the machine owns (the document keyboard listener, the pagehide and visibilitychange listeners, scheduled work), flushes a pending frecency save through the engine's existing path, and unregisters the leak sentinel; `resume()` re-attaches and re-arms; both are idempotent and no-ops on a disposed machine; state, registrations, the engine and the storage stay. The React root keeps one machine per mount in a null-guarded lazy `useRef` initializer (the one render-time ref write RA-58's source check exempts), suspends in its layout-effect cleanup and resumes in its layout-effect setup, so StrictMode's replay and an Activity hide/show keep the machine, its query, its registrations and its identity (CommandStableContext consumers see one machine). React's path never disposes terminally: a suspended machine holds no global reference, so a real unmount leaves it to the collector (the MEM-3 collection test of 60a9f96 is the proof), and an engine the machine created is released on collection through a FinalizationRegistry the machine registers at construction (or earlier by an explicit `machine[Symbol.dispose]` / `using`, which stays the documented teardown). Dispose stays terminal (CE-107, CE-113). The C1 manager designs the exact listener and timer inventory from the code and the verifier's F1 list, with a test per hold. LOCKED.

**D103 (orchestrator; extends D95 to search engines).** The machine disposes a search engine only if it created it: `ownsEngine = (options.searchEngine ?? options.search) === undefined`, mirroring FrecencyEngine's `#ownsStorage` (frecency/index.ts:84,233-235); a caller's engine is the caller's to dispose and survives the machine's dispose, suspend and resume. This is a 1.x behaviour change with no documented contract either way (types.ts:198, apps/docs/api/command-react.md:62 are silent on ownership), ruled here as the bug fix that makes DAE-64 and DAE-66 (1.7.0 docs rows: the samples that share or re-create an engine) true; the docs state the ownership rule where `search`/`searchEngine` is documented. Test: "D103: a caller's searchEngine is not disposed by the machine and keeps working after an Activity hide/show". LOCKED.

**Measured headroom (D99 input; corrects the plan's section 6, which cited the RC figures of ca9ae6f).** `pnpm exec size-limit --json` at b145c15: core 7383 B of 7400 (17 B headroom), react 5610 of 5700 (90 B), core+react 10816 of 10850 (34 B), react/dialog 1422 of 1500 (78 B). The frecency landings after the D90 RC measurement (G-49) account for the 62 B of core; the 1.6.0 RC size re-measure (RDG-128) records these at the 1.6.0 version bump. C1 therefore cannot land core without a D99 interim raise: the ruling is written at C1's landing from the measured tree, under the D99 ceilings. The verifier's byte sketch of the C1b additions as designed was +665 B gzip on the react entry; D102 removes the successor and seeding code from that sum, and D99's lever (the inert optimistic path replaced by plain sends) is applied in C1b before measurement.

### G-54 · 2026-11-08 · Wave 1 lands: C5 (CE-116, W05, W44 on the real wasm build), C7a (TEB-103 per-CPU baselines, the act guard, the quoted figures), the plan's C1 section as ruled; SEC-73 opened and fixed (the CI binary downloads); a correction to G-53's keyboard citation; the freshness sweep covers release-pinned tools

**C5 landed (16d5a9b, 81a0403, 4f04f68, 6277983; ledger f95ec0c; matrix 1a8f90f).** The main-thread wasm adapter keeps a `Map<ItemId, string>` of indexed items: `index()` upserts, `remove()` deletes only the given ids, `search()` returns only ids among the passed items (the SearchEngine contract of core/search/types.ts), and the crate limit is the whole index size because trigram.rs truncates after sorting. The opus-verifier's first round passed the semantics and found that re-serialising the map on every call made each `REGISTER_ITEM`/`UNREGISTER_ITEM` O(n): 10K single registrations took 73,344 ms on the real build and 10K unregistrations 76,912 ms. 4f04f68 rebuilds the crate index once before the next search after a change (a `stale` flag; 11 ms and 5 ms for the same 10K through the machine), and 6277983 closes the verifier's two test gaps (the remove tests search between `index()` and `remove()`; `stale` is cleared after the crate call, so a payload the crate rejects keeps throwing instead of serving the old index). The real-build tests (`wasm-engine.real.test.ts`) run against pkg/ and the `rust-wasm` CI job requires them (`MODERN_CMDK_REQUIRE_WASM_PKG=1`); that job is green on run 42468118182 with the real tests executing. Closing titles: CE-116 "CE-116: two index() batches through the machine are both searchable in the real wasm build", W05 "W05: remove() drops only the given ids", W44 "W44: search() returns only ids among the passed items" (its fixture `apple`/`appl`/`pple` makes the excluded item outscore the passed ones, so the limit mutation is red). Mutations each red on their test: batch-only serialisation, clear-on-remove, no re-sync, filter removed, limit `items.length`, `stale` not set by `index()` or `remove()`, `stale` cleared before the crate call.

**C7a landed (d6aafd0, 572d2a4, a46a6c5, 6882b59, 8492273; ledger 36ad510).** benchmarks/baseline.json is schemaVersion 2 with one record per CPU model and global thresholds (5 %/15 %/0.01 ms unchanged); the AMD EPYC 7763 record carries the previous numbers and meta byte-for-byte (deep-equal, and re-recording the committed history file reproduces the committed baseline). `bench:compare` applies WARN/FAIL only to the record of the run's CPU and prints INFO tables naming both CPUs otherwise (exit 0); `bench:record` writes only its file's CPU record and refuses a file without `environment.cpu`. D97's interim clause ends here: every later record is written by the orchestrator through `pnpm bench:record` from a fully green run (D93). The act guard: tests/act-guard.ts records a React act warning at the moment `console.error` is called and the setup file's `afterEach` fails the test; the verifier's first round found the guard blind to a warning swallowed by a test's own spy that is restored before that `afterEach` (all four spies in the repo are), so `silenceConsoleError()` replaces them and a source-scan test forbids any other spy on or assignment to `console.error` under tests/ and packages/*/src; React prints "suspended inside an act scope" once per module instance, so only the first offending test in a file goes red, as the comment now says. 6882b59 brings every quoted benchmark figure (CONTRIBUTING.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, apps/docs/benchmarks.md, apps/docs/guide/wasm-search.md, ci.yml, compare-baseline.ts) to the committed 7763 record (201f701, 2026-11-08): ts-scorer-10k 3.46 -> 3.79 ms median and 7.03 -> 7.92 p99, searchChange 0.37 -> 0.39 and 0.61 -> 0.72, navigate 0.75 -> 0.902 µs, the method sentence to D93's rule; the results JSON is schema 2. TEB-103 closes with four tests; the matrix carries none (TEST category).

The plan now reads D102/D103, the F4 to F8 corrections, the measured headroom at b145c15 and the C1 landing needing a D99 ruling. 1.7/plan and its worktree are removed (patch-equivalent).

The file has 142 lines: the constructor is at :32, `document.addEventListener('keydown', …)` at :127 and the `removeEventListener` at :132 (grep today).
