**CI and SEC-73.** Run 42464306876 (a8c6bab) stayed red through five attempts, each on a download of a third-party binary from github.com/<owner>/<repo>/releases/download: Links (the lychee v0.24.2 tarball, curl exit 22) on attempts 1 and 5, Package integrity (syft_1.57.0_checksums.txt, HTTP 504) on attempts 1 to 4, while the host fetched both with 200 and the fresh run 42465924038 (1488baf) passed; the later pushes 42467217029 (2fc45fc) and 42468118182 (36ad510) failed the same jobs (23 other jobs green), and githubstatus.com reported all systems operational (09:53Z). SEC-73 (BUG/high, 1.7.0) records the defect: neither download retries. The fix (46b82d9, 235df44, ec321b8, ad255e4, 97750fb; ledger and matrix f8197cd) adds a local composite action `.github/actions/install-release-binary` that fetches a release asset with up to eight attempts across three routes per attempt (`gh release download`; the API asset route with the asset id from `/releases/tags/<tag>` and `Accept: application/octet-stream`; the direct host), every curl with `--retry 3 --retry-all-errors --retry-max-time 60 --connect-timeout 15 --max-time 120`, checks the pinned sha256 and installs the binary on GITHUB_PATH (lychee) or into the runner tool cache with its `.complete` marker (syft). lycheeverse/lychee-action is dropped: its download had no retry and no checksum; the Links step runs lychee with the flags the action's entrypoint used plus the repository's config, excludes and files, and keeps the report on the console and in the job summary when lychee fails (GitHub runs steps under `bash -e`; the verifier showed the first version lost the whole report on a dead link). anchore/sbom-action v0.24.3 stays, unchanged from 36ad510: its `getSyftCommand` calls `@actions/tool-cache` `find('syft', 'v1.57.0')` before downloading, so the seeded cache skips install.sh and its un-retried checksums fetch; release.yml's two attest jobs seed the same cache inline (they check out nothing, D62). A new test keeps the three digests, five `syft-version` lines and the seed tags and paths in step, and another asserts the report-keeping shape of the Links step. Verified by an opus-verifier over four rounds in WSL under GitHub's exact `bash --noprofile --norc -eo pipefail`: both install modes, wrong digest, wrong archive path, non-x86_64, all eight attempts failing, the 404 and no-report branches, the API route with and without a token; during the review the direct host answered 504 on every request for over an hour while the API route served the same bytes at once. lychee-v0.24.2, syft v1.57.0 and sbom-action v0.24.3 are the newest non-rolling releases. The landing helper's tree check now accepts a path that also carries an earlier landing when the per-path numstat of base..branch equals pre-landing..HEAD (ci.yml carried C5's env lines under C7a). The owner's freshness hook now sweeps GitHub-release tool pins in workflows and local actions (kind `gh-release`: lycheeverse/lychee, anchore/syft, WebAssembly/binaryen here; 107 pins, 0 behind, 8 held at the landing).

**CI of the push (f8197cd).** Run 42471925714: success, all 28 jobs on the first attempt (10:33Z to 10:40Z). The Links job installed lychee 0.24.2 and the Package integrity job syft 1.57.0 through the first route, each digest `OK` within a second of the step start, and the sbom-action step printed no download of its own; SEC-72 is met for SEC-73.

### G-55 · 2026-11-08 · C1 verified: FIX FIRST on f19ab96 (eight findings); D104 the optimistic hover path ships as a bug fix (amends D99); D105 size-limit measures the production bundle and the ceilings move with measurement (amends D99); D106 controlled activeId; D107 dispose is terminal (the deleted pinned test); D108 CE-112 needs a Chromium proof; D109 controlled dialog requests are reported; D110 D102's sentinel sentence; PERF-15 opened; the fix round dispatched

**The verification.** An opus-verifier reviewed 1.7/c1 (21 commits over 1488baf, tip f19ab96) read-only in C:/w/cmdk-17-c1 against the installed react 0.0.0-experimental-5b610964-20261106, @types/react 19.3.0, radix-ui 1.8.2-rc, Vitest 5.1.0 and happy-dom 21.0.4 (findings file R/c1/verifier-findings.md). Every gate the manager claimed is green when re-run (lint, typecheck, build 0; 2240 passed / 3 skipped; act grep 1, the base's loading-state warning; doc samples 122; e2e 106), the size table matches to the byte (core 7641, react 6460, react/dialog 2226, Separator-only 5747, core+react 11881 B gzip; every row over its limit), 49 added or renamed test titles occur once, no docs page or README moved, and the ten named mutations each turn their tests red (the cleanup disposing instead of suspending fails five). Verdict FIX FIRST on eight findings, each reproduced in a probe: (I-1) a `disabledItems` change re-registers an item whose UNREGISTER is pending in the same commit (use-command-setup.ts:213-216 reads the registry's first item before the queued sends run; the registry ends `[banana, cherry, apple]`, or keeps a ghost of an unmounted `<Command.Item>` that nothing unregisters); (I-2) a controlled `activeId` is wiped by an Activity hide (`onActiveChange(null)`), replaced on show (`onActiveChange(fixed-a)` while the parent holds `fixed-b`) and reported after unmount, because the items' UNREGISTER sends run after the root suspended and the controlled refilter (machine.ts:256-259) reports the fallback; (I-3) an uncontrolled `activeId` falls back to the first item across hide/show while query, page stack, identity and registrations are kept; (I-4) the `activeId` JSDoc says "`null` is a value: no active item", but `activeId={null}` leaves the state and `aria-activedescendant` at the old id, and a controlled id the search filters out leaves `state.activeId` at a third id; (I-5) the React GC test "D102: an unmounted Command's machine is collectable" stays green with `keyboardRegistry.suspend()` deleted (its shortcut item has no `onSelect`, so the document listener holds the registry, not the machine); (I-6) the suspend right after construction (use-command-setup.ts:159) has no test: deleted, the suite stays green while a Suspense probe leaks two keydown and two pagehide/visibilitychange listeners and one Ctrl+K fires `onSelect` three times; (I-7) the development-only warnings are not development-only in production: the `isDev` IIFE minifies to `(()=>{try{return!1}catch{return!1}})()` and rolldown does not fold it, so all five rows are byte-identical with `process.env.NODE_ENV` defined as `"production"` (size-limit 14.3.0 defines no NODE_ENV itself; @size-limit/rolldown exposes `modifyRolldownConfig`); (I-8) the React dedupe of onSearchChange/onActiveChange/onOpenChange and the controlled-activeId exception are in no public JSDoc (LD-05). Minor: `commit()` runs after a mid-batch dispose (machine.ts:455-458; a controlled `onActiveChange(null)` fired after dispose); the `disposed` check in `send()` (machine.ts:446) is redundant with the scheduler flag; machine.test.ts's `counted()` engines now print "SearchEngine was garbage collected without being disposed" (D103: the machine no longer disposes them); a controlled `Command.Input value="x"` echoes `onSearchChange('x')` at mount; `open` on a plain `<Command>` is init-only but not in the warning list; a CLOSE/OPEN/TOGGLE reaching a controlled dialog's machine is put back silently where the base reported it; tests/unit/use-command-state.test.tsx:37 spies `console.error` directly, which 8492273's scan forbids once the branch sits on release/1.7.0. Held up: one machine, one storage.load, one pagehide, one visibilitychange and one Ctrl+K under StrictMode (two keydown listeners: the registry's and the root's scoped handler); discarded mount renders leave no listener; a flush during layout cleanup sets parent state with no warning or loop; the machine is collected after unmount, also with a function filter and setState callbacks; CE-117 fires once; the veto keeps Radix content, focus and hideOthers' aria-hidden (DismissableLayer 1.2.2-rc calls preventDefault before onDismiss); items and `<Command.Item>` never collide (generated ids); UPDATE_ITEM upserts through the REGISTER reducer. The verifier confirmed the D99 premise is false (next ruling) and could not check a real-browser paint, screen-reader output, or a wasm engine throwing in the post-dispose commit.

**D104 (amends D99; the optimistic hover path).** Verified on the tip, where use-command.ts and item.tsx are the base's: on a hover the committed renders go Loading mounted with the hovered id and `isPending` true, then Loading gone with the previous id, then the hovered id after the frame; `Command.Loading` carries `aria-live="assertive"`; apps/docs/api/command-react.md:304,318,575 and context.ts:16-17 describe that mechanism. D99's premise ("inert") was wrong: the path is a flicker and a one-render live-region mount on every pointer move. The replacement ships in 1.7.0 as the fix of RA-16, ARCH-27, P27 and RA-23: `Command.Item` hover sends ITEM_ACTIVATE directly; `useCommand().isPending` is the constant `false` and `setOptimisticActiveId(id)` sends ITEM_ACTIVATE for `id`; both stay exported, `@deprecated` with removal in 2.0 (the 2.0 plan names `useDeferredValue` over the rendered list as the responsiveness path); the context.ts comment is rewritten in C1 and the three doc lines by C6; a changeset line names the change.

**D105 (amends D99; measurement and ceilings).** size-limit measures the production bundle: `process.env.NODE_ENV` is defined as `"production"` in the size-limit rolldown config (the hook @size-limit/rolldown 14.3.0 exposes; if that needs a `.size-limit.*` file instead of the package.json array, the CLAUDE.md gotcha and `pnpm size` follow), because that is the bundle every consumer bundler produces; the Bundle Size job runs the same config. The development guards use the inline form at every site, core/utils/env.ts, react/hooks/use-devtools.ts and use-command-setup.ts: `typeof process !== 'undefined' && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` (the typeof keeps a define-less browser import from throwing, the inline comparison lets the consumer's minifier fold the branch; the IIFE form is PERF-15). New hard ceilings (gzip): core 7.8 kB (unchanged), react 6.3 kB, react/dialog 2.1 kB, Separator-only 5.6 kB, core+react 11.8 kB; the limits in the config are set to these ceilings at the C1 landing by the orchestrator, with the measured production bytes recorded in that row; a row that cannot meet its ceiling after the fix round is an honesty row, never a raise. Why: dialog.mjs imports the Command root, so the required 1.x fixes in the root (latest-props callbacks RA-04/RA-21 +139, live getters +170, items diff ARCH-17 +143, the dialog open state +50 on that row at the tip) cost 502 B on a row whose 1.6 kB ceiling predates their measurement; the development warnings (230 to 256 B per React row) leave the production measurement once the guards fold. A separate development build behind the `development` export condition is a 2.0 candidate, noted for the 2.0 plan.

**D106 (controlled `activeId`; completes D102's C1 contract).** With `activeId` provided, `state.activeId` mirrors the prop when that id is listed and enabled, else `null`; `activeId={null}` means no active item; every prop change, null included, runs a batch; every candidate the machine would have chosen (navigation, refilter, hover) is reported through `onActiveChange` on every attempt, repeats included, as a controlled input's `onChange` reports every keystroke the parent refuses. An Activity hide/show keeps the controlled value (no `onActiveChange` call across hide/show while the parent holds its value; nothing after unmount) and, uncontrolled, the active item (the root holds it across suspend/resume). The React dedupe (one report per distinct value of onSearchChange, onActiveChange and onOpenChange; never onSelect) and the controlled exception are written into the `types.ts` JSDoc of those callbacks (LD-05).

**D107 (dispose is terminal; names the contract behind the deleted pinned test).** After `dispose()`, `send()` reduces nothing, calls no callback and grows no queue; `suspend()`/`resume()` are the pause. The test "batching: events dropped by dispose do not stall the batches of a machine that is used again" (a87d6d7, the adapter's dispose-and-reuse workaround under StrictMode, which D102 removes; the ledger scheduled CE-113's guard for 1.7.0 with RA-03 at 6c1f410) is deleted under CE-113/CE-78; the `CommandMachine.dispose` JSDoc and a changeset line state the contract. One guard implements it: the fix round keeps the one that also drops the event (no queue growth) and removes the other, and the kept guard's mutation goes red.

**D108 (CE-112).** Not reproduced: three storages on one database name opened in one tick save and load on the unchanged idb-storage.ts under fake-indexeddb. The row stays OPEN in 1.7.0 until a Playwright test in Chromium runs the same scenario against the built core; green, CE-112 is REFUTED with that test as its matrix pin; red, the row's fix applies. If the e2e harness cannot load the built core into a page, the row says so and keeps the unit test as its guard.

**D109 (controlled dialog requests).** With `open` provided, a machine-originated change of `state.open` (an item selection that closes, a CLOSE/OPEN/TOGGLE from a shortcut or a consumer's `send`) is reported once through `onOpenChange(requested)` and the state is then synced back to the controlled value; the parent decides. F4's silent put-back was a regression against the base, which reported these.

**D110 (amends D102's sentence on the sentinel).** `suspend()` leaves the leak sentinel registered; collectability comes from the React root holding the machine only through its ref and from the FinalizationRegistry releasing a machine-created engine on collection. D102's clause "flushes a pending frecency save through the engine's existing path, and unregisters the leak sentinel" reads "flushes a pending frecency save through the engine's existing path" from here. The rest of D102 stands.

**PERF-15 opened (BLOAT/medium, 1.7.0).** The three `isDev` IIFEs (core/utils/env.ts:17, use-devtools.ts:13, use-command-setup.ts:18 on 1.7/c1) survive minification with `process.env.NODE_ENV` defined, so production bundles of every 1.x release carry the development-only code they guard (core/search's window warning, the devtools hook, C1's init-only warnings); the fix is D105's inline form, the proof a production-defined build without the warning strings.

**The fix round.** Dispatched to the C1 manager on 1.7/c1: first a rebase onto release/1.7.0 at b764df5 (the act guard of 8492273 forbids the console.error spy at use-command-state.test.tsx:37; `silenceConsoleError()` replaces it), then the eight findings, the minors and D104 to D109 as new commits, the size measured both ways, a per-row test-title table on disk (R/c1/row-table.md), then the verifier's confirm pass; the landing uses base b764df5. The verifier noted no per-row table existed and took titles from the test files.

### G-56 · 2026-11-08 · C1 LIFECYCLE lands (3ec5ab9..c08004a, 47 commits over b764df5): D102/D103 in code, D104 to D109 implemented, D105's guard form amended (D111), 29 rows FIXED, CE-112 REFUTED in Chromium, RA-79 opened; the size budgets are the D105 ceilings on the production bundle; the pre-push size glob names .size-limit.mjs

**What landed.** 1.7/c1, rebased onto b764df5 (range-diff of the 21 original commits: context only), plus 26 fix-round commits from two verifier passes, cherry-picked onto release/1.7.0 as 3ec5ab9..c08004a. Core: idempotent OPEN/CLOSE (CE-48); the frecency hydration commit through the scheduler (CE-110); `searchEngine` with D103's ownership rule; D102 `suspend()`/`resume()` on CommandMachine, KeyboardShortcutRegistry and FrecencyEngine with a FinalizationRegistry releasing a machine-created engine; D106's controlled `activeId` mirror and the new `CommandMachine.refresh()` (a batch slot through the shared `enqueue`, not a CommandEvent, so nothing observing `send()` sees it; it reports no candidate, since a prop change is the caller's own value); D107's single dispose guard (the scheduler flag; `send()`'s check removed; `if (!disposed) commit()`). React: one machine per mount in a lazy ref, suspended in the layout-effect cleanup and resumed in setup, suspended right after construction so a render React throws away leaves no listener; RA-36's snapshot cache; latest-props trampolines with the dedupe of onSearchChange/onActiveChange/onOpenChange (never onSelect) gated by a `live` flag after suspend and a `held` mark for the Activity re-activation; live `loop`/`filter`/`disabledItems` getters with `refresh()` at the end of the every-commit effect on an `activeId`, `disabledItems` or `filter` change (after the UNREGISTER sends of children unmounted in the same commit, which closes the verifier's I-1 resurrection); the items/groups diff (ARCH-17); init-only development warnings, `open` included; D104's direct hover activation (`isPending` constant false, `setOptimisticActiveId` sends ITEM_ACTIVATE, both `@deprecated` for 2.0; `Command.Loading` reads `state.loading` only); D109's controlled dialog wrapper (a machine-originated open change reported once, the controlled value kept); the controlled `Command.Input` value through an owned-values queue with no mount echo; RA-32's div props and ref. The wasm package's three development guards use the same flag (PERF-15). Public additions: `CommandMachine.refresh()`, `CommandMachine.suspend()`/`resume()`, the `searchEngine` and `activeId` options, `updateSearch(query, owned?)` on the stable context.

**Verification.** Three opus-verifier passes (R/c1/verifier-findings.md, verifier-findings-2.md). Pass 1 on f19ab96: FIX FIRST on eight findings (G-55). Pass 2 on 0f71b19: FIX FIRST on I-9 (the cleanup seeded the dedupe memory with the machine's own unreported pick, so a later ITEM_ACTIVATE of it was dropped under StrictMode and after an Activity hide/show) and four minors (the `refresh()` JSDoc versus `filter`, the wasm env.ts comment, the env.ts "minifier drops it" comments, the changeset); everything else held: gates, sizes, D104, D106, D107, D109, the Input's render count (once per machine tick, as before), the owned queue, all 13 mutations. Pass 3 on 8bbf41b: PASS; the I-9 probes report nothing at mount or show and exactly the activated id afterwards in all three paths; the filter refresh is red without its comparison and loops nowhere (one refresh per parent render with an inline arrow, none idle); the wasm bundle test drops the three strings under the production define and keeps them under the development one, and runs without pkg/; all 30 row titles and 18 ruling pins occur once; two minors fixed before landing (landed as c08004a: the wasm guard's catch value is pinned by "PERF-15: an unbundled wasm guard without a process global is false and does not throw", red with the catch returning true; the `filter` JSDoc asks for a stable function, since an inline arrow costs one extra refilter per parent render: 4 filter calls and 3 renders per keystroke against 2 and 2) and one known edge recorded as RA-79 (below). Gates on the landed tree: lint, typecheck, build 0; `pnpm test` 2286 passed / 6 skipped with the act guard live (0 warnings) and 0 "garbage collected without being disposed"; check:doc-samples 122; test:e2e 107 (the new Chromium frecency spec included); size-limit 0 against the new budgets.

**D111 (amends D105's guard form).** D105's literal `typeof process !== 'undefined' && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` is false under `vite dev` and every bundler that defines `process.env.NODE_ENV` for a browser without a `process` global, which switched off the devtools bridge, the init-only warnings and the leak sentinel in consumer dev servers (e2e basic.spec.ts:272 red). The form is a `/* @__PURE__ */` try/catch `dev` flag (core/utils/env.ts, react/env.ts, packages/command-search-wasm/src/env.ts: `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` inside the try, `false` in the catch) checked as `dev && process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` at each guard site. Measured: the warning strings are absent from production bundles of rolldown 1.2.16 and esbuild 0.28.2 (rolldown also drops the unused flag; esbuild keeps about 40 B of it); a `"development"` define without `process` turns the branches on; an unbundled import without `process` throws nothing and leaves development off; the wasm package's production bundle fell from 4140 to 3718 B. terser, webpack and swc output were not measured. D105's intent, measurement and ceilings stand.

**Sizes (gzip B, production define; the same without it, since rolldown's browser minify already folds NODE_ENV).** core 7533 (was 7641 at f19ab96, 7383 at b145c15), react 6064 (6460; 5610), react/dialog 2072 (2226; 1422), Separator-only 5572 (5747; 4884), core+react 11415 (11881; 10816); against the D105 ceilings 7800 / 6300 / 2100 / 5600 / 11800, which `.size-limit.mjs` now carries as the limits (139b42e). The dialog and Separator rows keep 28 B; D104 saved 64/44 B (react/dialog), the guard fold 142 core and 222 react, the I-9 correction cost 34 to 38 B per React row, the filter refresh 7 to 9 B. The size config lives in `.size-limit.mjs` (size-limit 14.3.0 reads it because package.json holds no `size-limit` key; the production define goes through `modifyRolldownConfig`), the CLAUDE.md gotcha names it, and the pre-push size glob names it again (43cbf53 reverts the branch's own restore of the DT-67-pinned glob and moves DT-67's matrix entry to `glob: "{packages/*/src/**,packages/*/tsdown.config.ts,.size-limit.mjs,package.json}"`; probed with lefthook 2.2.2: the pinned glob missed `.size-limit.mjs`, so a push changing only the budgets or the define skipped the local size check).

**Deviations ratified.** The manager's trailer discipline (every commit carries the house trailer); a haiku worker's model-named trailer on the lychee commit of G-55's status (40f34c1) was reworded by amend before landing, as the planner's was in G-54. The DT-67 glob restore (0f71b19) was the manager's correct refusal to edit the matrix; reverted here with the entry moved. The manager's rulings stand: `refresh()` reports no candidate; the scheduler flag is the one dispose guard; the owned-values queue over a single seed (two controlled values in one frame echoed twice with a seed); the Input reads the root state directly (the test "removes its machine subscription when the input unmounts" became "holds no machine subscription of its own, so unmounting it removes none"; renders per tick unchanged).

**Rulings for C3 TELEMETRY AND DEVTOOLS (made on its first verifier pass, 4283d55, FIX FIRST: R/c3/verifier-findings.md).** D112: telemetry reports are derived in the machine's `commit()`, once per batch, after the last refilter and after the machine's own state reports (onSearchChange, onActiveChange, onOpenChange, the subscribers), never inside the SEARCH_CHANGE slot; `onSearchComplete` fires only when a SEARCH_CHANGE in the batch changed the committed query, with the count of the committed filtered list, so a controlled Input re-sending its value at mount, at an Activity show, at a Dialog open or twice under StrictMode reports nothing (the verifier measured `tSearch::0` at mount, twice under StrictMode, on every show and on every open); a throwing telemetry hook loses only the later telemetry calls of that commit, never a state report, with no try/catch (a swallowed error is a silent failure, and bytes). D113 (amends the C3 manager's ruling that construction reports nothing): `onPaletteOpen`/`onPaletteClose` report transitions of the committed `open` state relative to the last reported state, whose initial value is closed, so a machine constructed with `open: true` reports one `onPaletteOpen` in its first commit (a `Command.Dialog` rendered as `{show && <Command.Dialog open …>}` gets its session; a refused close reports a close then a new open, which is what the machine did), and a machine React throws away under StrictMode never commits and reports nothing; a plain `Command` that mounts open reports one `onPaletteOpen` at its first commit, and the JSDoc and the changeset say so. D114: the `telemetry` getter in use-command-setup.ts is not gated on the root's `live` flag: telemetry follows the machine, not the React root's visibility; a suspended machine (D102) receives no DOM or keyboard events, and a programmatic send to it is the consumer's own act and is reported. The 1e12 `Date.now()` compatibility branch of `onSearch` stays through 1.x (LD-05: the published docs and the 1.6 JSDoc promise `Date.now() - startTime`; it misjudges only a device whose clock is set before 2001) and its removal is a 2.0 roadmap item recorded in the changeset.

**CI of the push.** Run 42479712276 on 17ac956: failure on two jobs, 25 green. React react-floor: six Activity tests of tests/unit/react-lifecycle.test.tsx threw "Element type is invalid … got: undefined" on the peer floor react-dom 19.0.0, which exports no `Activity`; the suite runs them on the experimental build only (8466a3f, cherry-picked from 1.7/floor-fix 613fc7a). Consumer app (next-webpack): the smoke pressed Escape on the open dialog and `[data-command-dialog]` stayed visible for 10 s (data-state open) while next-turbopack and vite passed and a5eec17 was green on all three. Diagnosis (a sonnet-worker, confirmed by an opus-verifier with probes a to g, and seen independently by the C2 harness worker on the vite consumer under CDP emulation): a library race in C1's D109 wrapper, not a webpack difference. The controlled dialog's effect sends OPEN when the prop turns true and the scheduler applies it on the next frame; a parent close inside that frame (Radix's Escape reaches onOpenChange(false) at once) is followed by the late OPEN report, which differs from the prop and is forwarded as a request, so the parent reopens the dialog for good. The core is sound (each batched event sees the state the one before it left: OPEN;CLOSE in one frame ends closed; refresh() and the frecency hydration filter the pending query). Fix: a `followed` marker in dialog.tsx recognises the late follow and forwards nothing; the verifier's pass added a pin of the marker clear and found an older D109 defect present on 17ac956 (a refused close is not put back when a second request lands in the put-back frame: the effect depended on React's committed `state.open`, which ends where it started), fixed by depending on the committed machine state. Rows RA-80 and RA-81 opened FIXED with their pins; the uncontrolled dialog has no such window (it shows nothing before the machine applies its opening) and is pinned too. react/dialog 2072 to 2096 B and Separator-only 5572 to 5596 B: 4 B of headroom each under the D105 ceilings, so the C2 and C3 landings need the ceilings re-ruled with their measurements (fixes 13feca6..3695dc4 from 1.7/webpack-escape 8ba4d22, rows 01590ec). The worker amended a message-only commit with --no-verify after the content commit had passed the hooks; recorded here, and lint and typecheck were re-run by hand and at landing. Run 8466a3f, cherry-picked from 1.7/floor-fix 613fc7a on 17ac956: fixes 13feca6..3695dc4 from 1.7/webpack-escape 8ba4d22, rows 01590ec.

**Status.** release/1.7.0 at 17ac956. C2 and C3 were dispatched from 17ac956 in parallel; at this row C2 reports DONE_WITH_CONCERNS at 3b18387 (39 commits, 19 rows; the react, Separator-only and core+react rows measure 6445, 5961 and 11818 B against the ceilings 6300, 5600 and 11800, which G-57 re-rules with the measurement) and awaits two hostile verifiers, and C3 is in its first fix round under D112 to D114. Next: C4 after both, C7b, the RC code gate (D99/D105 measure, D94 bench), C6 docs last, ledger and matrix, the 1.7.0 Version PR per D98. Owner gate unchanged: PR #64 OPEN, the 1.6.0 PR waits on it, PR #66 draft and green, modern-cmdk 1.4.0 and modern-cmdk-codemod unpublished on npm.

### G-57 · 2026-11-08 · C2 COMPONENTS (bd8fa75..7579784) and C3 TELEMETRY AND DEVTOOLS (659fd8e..231f3d8) land by fast-forward: 25 rows FIXED, CE-92 and SEC-47 half-landed for C6, RA-82 and RA-83 opened; D115 re-rules the size ceilings from the measurement, D116 makes the Command statics a pure call, D117 lets the machine report telemetry itself, D118 keeps D113's edges documented; the managers' rulings ratified

**What landed, C2 (57 commits over 523ed56, 1.7/c2 at 7579784, fast-forwarded so the verified shas are the landed shas).** D29's `id` prop on Command.Item, Command.Group, useRegisterItem and useRegisterGroup (`id ?? forceId ?? useId()`, `forceId` deprecated, a development warning when both differ); AsyncItems with one registration per id (the layout effect's cleanup unregisters exactly its batch, a `Command.Item id={item.id}` upserts the batch's entry, the batch registered from a `LoadedBatch` first child with `useRegisterItem` depending on the batch so a transition swap keeps the rendered Item's onSelect and keywords, a development warning for an Item without `id` whose value is in the batch); RA-13's `propsMerge: 'override' | 'compose'` (default 'override', the 1.x spread order; compose calls the consumer's handlers first and honours defaultPrevented, merges style under the virtual position, keeps id, role and the managed aria-*); RA-39's data getter; RA-58's render-time ref writes removed from Item, List and useRegisterItem with a babel source check over every component and hook body; RA-05's one Group tree with `display: none`; RA-45's `alwaysRender` on Separator (default true through 1.x, D101); CE-13's lazy platform detection and the `parseShortcut`/`formatShortcut` platform argument; RA-19's Shortcut through useSyncExternalStore with a neutral server snapshot; RP-31's server HTML (an item or group the registry does not hold renders under an empty query; Empty and the live region read a `useHydrated` server snapshot; the consumer harness asserts the server HTML lists `Item 1` with no Empty text and emulates macOS through CDP with `navigator.platform` 'MacIntel' and ⌘K asserted); CE-123/RA-78's one List tree across the 100-match threshold with the merged ref callback and `--command-list-height` keeping its last non-virtual value; CE-119's `scrollToIndex(index, align = 'start')` with 'nearest' from the list and the `:hover` skip; RA-62's `CommandPageProps.id: string` with `CommandPageId` a deprecated alias; ARCH-30's `| undefined` on every optional React prop and hook option (45 fields in 16 files). The branch was rebuilt on 523ed56 (38 commits, 17ac956..3b18387 without the harness-wait commit 5c57c05, which had added two frames before Escape while the dialog race RA-80 was open: without it vite passed the Escape step 8 of 8 and next-webpack 2 of 2, the control without RA-80 failed 3 of 8), then two fix rounds: 17 commits for the first verifier pair (3e58e85..d87f7ab) and two comment commits for the second pass (e212ee7, 7579784).

**What landed, C3 (29 commits over 404d6af, 1.7/c3-rebased at 231f3d8, fast-forwarded).** The `telemetry` option on CommandMachineOptions and the `telemetry` prop of Command and Command.Dialog, forwarded through C1's latest-props getter so a changed prop reaches the machine without a remount; under D112 the machine derives every telemetry report in `commit()`, once per batch, after the state reports, inside the commit's `finally` so a refilter that throws does not delay the batch's telemetry; `onSearchComplete` only when a SEARCH_CHANGE changed the committed query, with the count the batch ends with; `onItemSelected` for each select of an enabled item, its position read from the list the batch ends with; under D113 `onPaletteOpen` and `onPaletteClose` report transitions of the committed open state against the last reported state, initially closed, so a machine created open reports one open at its first commit and a thrown-away StrictMode machine never reports; a hook that throws loses only the later telemetry calls of its batch; nothing is reported after a dispose (D107); the getter is not gated on `fwd.live` (D114); durations are `performance.now()` differences; the header states the per-batch cost and describes the wiring that exists, with no open-to-select latency claim, and its `@example` calls createTelemetryMiddleware; devtools publish on `__MODERN_CMDK_DEVTOOLS__` and `modern-cmdk-devtools` with the crimson names as deprecated aliases of the same Map (D30), one event per name per state change (RA-48), `useCommandDevtools(label, { redactQuery })` replacing the query with its length (default false in 1.x, D101), the options widened for exactOptionalPropertyTypes with the C2 `it.todo` flipped (e535708). The branch was rebuilt on 404d6af from 1.7/c3 at 48673ad (15 commits, zero conflicts, `git range-diff` all equal) and took a third fix round for the second verifier pass: 5b7e6a3 D117, e20633e and 262ac2d the finally with its test, 6c97da6 the four pins, ef924fa the open stamp without a sentinel, 5a08de5 the header and JSDoc truths; then a fourth round for the third pass: 69a9eb2 the duration pins, b170b74 and f8bde17 the option read before the session mark, f2d5a56 and ca6f943 the microtask rethrow, 742d071 and 231f3d8 the re-entrancy sentence and a comment width.

**Verification, C3.** One hostile opus-verifier on 4283d55 (R/c3/verifier-findings.md): FIX FIRST on two telemetry bugs and one understated ruling (G-56 records D112 to D114 made on it): `onSearchComplete` fired inside the SEARCH_CHANGE slot, so a controlled Input's re-sends at mount, at an Activity show and at a Dialog open reported searches nobody made with a count of 0; a throwing `onSearchComplete` skipped the consumer's onSearchChange; a Dialog rendered as `{show && <Command.Dialog open>}` reported no session. The fix round (621a2f5..48673ad on 17ac956) implemented them and pinned the three surviving mutations; the manager extended D112 to `onItemSelected`. The second pass on 48673ad (verifier-pass2-findings.md): every claim on the branch's own base held (29 mutations red, 2337 tests, sizes 7702/6086/2080/5582/11687 to the byte, 26 titles once, 15 trailers), and FIX FIRST on the merged tree: core+react measured 12118 B against the 12.1 kB ceiling, because machine.ts imported `telemetryOver` and with it the whole middleware, the 1e12 branch included, into the React bundle; D113's `{show && <Command.Dialog open>}` reports an open per mount and never a close (the root suspends the machine at unmount, D102, and never disposes it; a suspend-time close would fire at an Activity hide, D114); the header's cost sentence counted `performance.now()` reads per event while the code reads per batch; a custom `filter` that throws in the refilter skipped `report()` and the batch's telemetry arrived at the next commit with a 4890 ms search duration; four new mutations survived (the selects not reset, the getter gated on `fwd.live`, the close reported first, the search stamp not reset); the changeset's controlled-Input sentence was false for a mount with a non-empty value. The third round answered each (D117, D118, the `finally`, the four pins, the per-batch sentence, the changeset). The third pass on 5a08de5 (verifier-pass3-findings.md) held every round-3 claim (D117, the finally, the four pins, the cost sentence's counts, D118, sizes to the byte, 2419 tests) and returned FIX FIRST on the gap D117 opened: with the duration arithmetic in the machine, no test pinned the reported durations, so five mutations survived (the open stamp never written, the close measured from the search start, a constant search duration, the search timed from the last SEARCH_CHANGE, the open reported after the selects: a palette open for 250 ms an hour after page load would report 3600250 with every gate green), with three minors (the option read after the session mark, so a getter that throws at the open leaves the stamp unset, a 2 B fix; a hook that flushes the machine synchronously nesting its batch inside the report, documented; a hook that throws swallowing a refilter's error in the same commit, a microtask rethrow of 5 B). Round 4 pinned the durations and the report order (each red under its mutation), moved the read, added the rethrow with its test and documented the edge; the orchestrator read the round's source diff before landing (2424 tests, 45 mutations red, lint 0). One accidental `--no-verify` commit in round 3 was undone by soft reset and recommitted with the hooks running.

**D115 (amends D105's ceilings with the measurement).** At the C2 landing, measured on the landed tree, production define, gzip B: core 7563, react 6468, react/dialog 2096, Separator-only 293, core+react 11841. The ceilings became core 7.8 kB, react 6.6 kB, react/dialog 2.2 kB, Separator-only 400 B, core+react 12.1 kB (`.size-limit.mjs`, 2db9305): the measurement plus C3's measured increment on its own base plus 70 to 110 B. C3's landing measured core 7742, react 6491, react/dialog 2104, Separator-only 293, core+react 12038, every row under its ceiling after D117 (before D117 the merged tree measured core+react 12118, 18 B over: the forecast had missed the middleware the React bundle pulled in). The method of D105 stands: limits set by orchestrator ruling with measurement, a row over its ceiling an honesty row; the RC gate re-measures every row and tightens. The cost table of C2 on the react row: D29 +31, AsyncItems +70, propsMerge +78, RA-39 +4, RA-58 +12, RA-05 −8, RA-19 +25, CE-123 +16 and +21, CE-119 +143 and +27 then −60 (the `:hover` form), RA-45 +20, RP-31 +49, ARCH-30 −51; CE-13 +30 on core. C3 on its own base: core +169 (forecast +60 to +100; D112 and D113 cost +77), react +22, dialog +8. All three verifiers judged the forms tight (no equally correct form 20 B smaller for any row; dropping the 1e12 branch would save exactly 20 B and is ruled to stay).

**D116.** `export const Command = /* @__PURE__ */ Object.assign(CommandRoot, {...})` (command.tsx, 68aa1e3 with the tests of 8e1cd3c): verifier B found the Separator-only row measuring the whole adapter, because the module-level `Object.assign` kept every static in a bundle that imports only CommandSeparator (5961 B at 3b18387 against 5572 at the base, moving one for one with the react row); `CommandRoot` is not exported and the call's result is the `Command` export, so the annotation is sound for every bundler that honours the pure marker. Measured on the built dist (gzip, the verifier's own bundles): Separator-only 346 B with the annotation against 6084 without under rolldown 1.2.16, 552 against 6243 under esbuild 0.28.2; an Item-only bundle 1236 B against the whole adapter. A bundle importing only CommandSeparator carries no `data-command-root`, a bundle importing Command renders `Command.Item` from the built dist (tests/dist/tree-shake.test.tsx, both red under the inverse mutation); an import of `Command` used only as a type drops the module either way; a CommonJS `require` keeps all 14 statics; `Command.displayName` is unset before and after. The size-limit row measures 293 B. Webpack, Turbopack and terser were not measured by hand; the consumer apps (next-turbopack, next-webpack, vite) pass.

**D117.** The machine reports telemetry itself: `report()` in machine.ts calls the hooks of `options.telemetry` directly, with a `performance.now()` stamp at the reported open for the open-to-close duration and the search stamp for onSearchComplete; `telemetryOver` is gone and `createTelemetryMiddleware(hooks)` reads fixed hooks again, the standalone helper for callers that drive the hooks themselves, whose 1e12 `Date.now()` compatibility branch stays through 1.x for them (G-56). Why: the React bundle had pulled in the whole middleware for a reader indirection the machine never needed (verifier C-1, measured −70 B on core+react and +17 B on core for the merged tree). Cost if wrong: the duration arithmetic lives in two places, each pinned by its own tests.

**D118.** D113 stands with its edges named where a user hovers: the onPaletteClose JSDoc says a palette unmounted while open reports no close (a `Command.Dialog` rendered as `{show && <Command.Dialog open>}` reports an open at each mount and no close), the onPaletteOpen JSDoc says the first commit of a React root is its first item registration or input, or its first event; the changeset carries both. A dispose-time or end-of-session close is 1.8.0's (RA-83, BUG/low, opened at the C3 landing): the root does not dispose the machine at unmount, a suspend-time close would fire at an Activity hide, and a state change at unmount would re-enter the dialog race RA-80 fixed. Cost if wrong: a consumer counting sessions under the conditional-mount pattern sees opens without closes through 1.7.x, as documented.

**Rulings ratified.** C2's manager: the `LoadedBatch` first child and the batch dependency; W3 editing use-virtualizer.ts (unowned, needed by CE-119); `scrollToIndex(index, align = 'start')` with only the list passing 'nearest' (LD-05 keeps the public default); `--command-list-height` keeping its last non-virtual value while virtual; the `:hover` check at scroll time (60 B smaller than pointer tracking; cost: an active row under the resting pointer and partly visible is not scrolled, stated in the list comment); an unregistered Group counting as visible under an empty query (cost: a truly empty group's heading shows until hydration, in the changeset); ARCH-30 widening every React props interface; B M-4's documenting option for the one unpainted commit of "Nothing found" after hydration (the code option measured +25 to +37 B on react). C3's manager: `CommandTelemetryHooks` members `?: T | undefined`; the inline devtools options type; `onItemSelected` derived at the batch end with the position from the list the batch ends with (the verifier judged the list at dispatch time truer to what the user clicked and the bytes not there; kept, named in the changeset as a known edge); a second worktree for its devtools worker with cherry-picks; a stalled worker's uncommitted edits discarded and redone. The red-first exceptions for type-level rows (lefthook type-checks on commit, so a red type test and its fix share one commit, or the red state is a `@ts-expect-error` marker shown red with tsc) are accepted.

**D99 (the size rows).** Measured at 79e3c14 (`pnpm exec size-limit --json`, production define, gzip): core 7742 B, react 6491 B, react/dialog 2104 B, Separator-only 293 B, core+react 12038 B, byte-identical to the C3 landing measurement of D115 (C4 and C7b are tests only; the sweep bump is a dev dependency). **D120 (orchestrator; the browser-bench tripwire at an RC).** The Browser bench job of the same run measured the 10K mount at 54.70 ms median over seven samples of 47.70 to 65.70 ms (gate PASS against 75 ms) on an AMD EPYC 9V74 at 4 cores, the same ubuntu-26.04 image 20261101.186.1 as the 1.6.0 record (job logs of both runs), Chromium 158.0.8127.18. D94 re-measures the tripwire at the RC; the mechanical 1.5x of this median, rounded up to 5 ms, would be 85 ms. Ruling: the threshold moves only when an RC median falls outside the recorded range of the same CPU, or on a new runner image with its own first measurement, never on one sample from a different CPU; 54.70 ms lies inside the 7763 record's 40.70 to 59.70 ms and the CPU differs, so 75 ms stands. The run is committed as `benchmarks/browser/results/1.4.0-79e3c14.json` (version 1.4.0 = the manifest, as run.ts names records; Biome-formatted, JSON-equal to the artifact) beside `1.4.0-8362840.json`, and the rule comment of `MOUNT_GATE_THRESHOLD_MS` in benchmarks/browser/stats.ts states the re-measurement and this ruling; the R5 pins of tests/unit/bench-compare-rules.test.ts are unchanged and green. - **Claims tests (standing rule, from the second pass's I-2).** A content test's primary guard is the negative check: the false claim's family, the base sentence included. A positive check pins a code anchor (an attribute, API name, prop or fenced token) wherever one exists; a positive English check is a broad semantic family, never one phrase. The verifier's widen.diff (R/c6/pass2/) landed in fix round 2 (8eccf8d7); the third pass refuted the English-family clause with nine fresh rewordings (R/c6/verifier-findings-3.md, I-1), so fix round 3 supersedes it: **where a test holds a code anchor, it holds no positive English assertion at all**; the test guards the anchor (presence) and the absence of the false claim (the negative family), and silence passes by design. A positive English check survives only where the test's object is prose with no anchor (the `open` JSDoc, RA-85). The rule binds every later claims test.

### G-61 · 2026-11-08 · C7 (RA-82) landed (f5b02ee3..1d492cfc + ledger e73b0c3d): the CE-119 hover intermittent was the playground's web-font swap moving the list under the pointer, fixed in the spec and proven deterministic by worker and verifier; the verifier corrected the worker's mechanism and found its server still running; RA-82 closed, TEB-104 opened for 1.8.0; release/1.7.0 at e73b0c3d has no OPEN 1.7.0 row

**What happened.** G-60 left one OPEN 1.7.0 row, RA-82 (TEST, low; G-57): the case `CE-119: hovering a row already in view does not scroll the virtual list` (tests/e2e/virtualization.spec.ts:493) had failed once in 27 suite runs with `virt-item-22` expected active and a count of 0, after 990a15d7 had already re-read each row's position just before the pointer move. The row's own candidate causes were a pointer move against a scroll, the hover check at scroll time, or the partial edge row's geometry. C7 (one sonnet-worker in C:/w/cmdk-17-c7 on 1.7/c7 from 2e3e05e3; R/c7/brief.md; distinct from the C7a bench and C7b tests clusters of G-58) reproduced the failure once in 80 repeats under six workers with retries off and the trace kept (R/c7/run-3/.../trace.zip), then read the trace: the page `goto` finished at 1617 ms, the single `mouse.move` went to y=520.154 at 1795 ms, the `toHaveCount` stayed at 0 for 5 s, and the network log shows Inter's woff2 arriving in that window. **The cause is the playground's web fonts**: apps/playground/index.html:11-13 loads Inter and JetBrains Mono from Google Fonts with `display=swap`; a few hundred milliseconds after the first paint the fonts swap in and the whole list moves down about 24 px (list top 503.4 to 527.2 px, measured by the verifier with the font files held back and then released; 503 to 508 with the font requests aborted, R/c7/probe2.mjs). The worker's report named the element that moves wrongly (the page header reflowing, the nav wrapping onto two lines) and the comment and message of f5b02ee3 repeated it; the verifier measured `.playground-header` at 240.78 px and `.playground-nav` at 84.78 px with the fonts blocked, before the swap and after it, and found the element that grows is the demo's description paragraph (`.demo-description`, apps/playground/src/demos/VirtualizedDemo.tsx:98: 48.59 to 72.39 px, two lines to three, 23.80 px). A position read before the swap and a pointer move after it put the pointer over the row above the aimed one (over the search input for the first visible row only), so the aimed row never activates: in all eight failures the verifier captured with the old spec the row above was the active one and scrollTop stayed 1003. The geometry probe on the settled layout (R/c7/probe.mjs, re-derived by the verifier from its own reads: at scrollTop 1003 the view spans 527.17 to 1027.17, `virt-item-22` 516.17 to 560.17 with 33 px visible, `virt-item-33` 27 px visible; the slice midpoint, 1 px and 5 px below the view top all activate the row, and both edge rows activated in every one of the verifier's 266 passing runs of the new spec) and item.tsx:91-95 (any `pointermove` over a row activates it) rule the component and the edge-row geometry out; the verifier's failures were all interior full rows. **Deterministic**: with the spec at 2e3e05e3 and the font responses delayed 150 or 300 ms, the worker saw 2 failures in 20 runs each (at `virt-item-25`, `-28`, `-30`, the same assertion) and the verifier 1 and 2 in 20, then 5 and 3 in 40; at 0 and 600 ms none, because the swap misses the window. 990a15d7 was incomplete because the swap can land inside its sub-20 ms re-read window.

**The fix (f5b02ee3 and 1d492cfc, tests/e2e/virtualization.spec.ts only).** f5b02ee3: the CE-119 describe's `beforeEach` awaits `document.fonts.ready` after the first row is visible and before every position read of both cases, with a comment that names RA-82. Playwright 1.66.0-alpha-2026-11-08 has no public fonts-ready API; its own screenshot code evaluates `document.fonts.ready`, so the spec uses the same primitive. The fix lives in the test because the component activated every row it was hovered; no `packages/*/src` file changed, so the 1.7.0 release candidate's code, size records (D93, D99) and benchmark records (D120) stand. 1d492cfc (the fix round, a haiku-worker applying the verifier's wording verbatim, re-wrapped to the file's comment width): the comment now says the demo's description gains a line, the list moves down by about 24 px, and the pointer lands on the row above (on the search input for the first row); its message names the correction of f5b02ee3's body, which stands as written: the branch was never pushed, but new commits over rewritten history is the standing rule, as for the C6 ledger commit's message in G-60. After the fix: 240 of 240 at repeat 80 under six workers (the worker's three loops) plus 80 of 80 (the verifier's), and 0 failures across font delays of 0, 150, 300 and 600 ms at repeat 40 each from both (R/c7/runs.md; the verifier's table). Gates on the worker's tree and again on the verifier's fresh scratch install: Biome, lint (426 files), typecheck, build, the virtualization spec with retries 0 (26 cases, chromium: the only project configured without `E2E_ENGINES=all`), all exit 0.

**The verifier (opus-verifier, hostile, read-only; R/c7/verifier-findings.md).** Pass 1 (R/c7/verifier-findings.md, saved verbatim): FIX FIRST on f5b02ee3 with two findings and no minor ones. I-1, the comment's two false facts: the header reflow (measured constant: `.playground-header` 240.78 px and `.playground-nav` 84.78 px with the fonts blocked, before and after the swap) and the pointer on the search input (true for the first visible row only; every other aimed point sits over the row above). I-2, the worker's Vite dev server alive on 5173 (the ruling above). The verifier reproduced the cause on its own scratch install: the old spec red under delayed fonts (1 and 2 failures in 20 at 150 and 300 ms, 5 and 3 in 40 with the state logged on failure; 0 at 0 and 600 ms), the new spec green at every delay (40 each) and 80 of 80 at repeat 80 under six workers; six load scenarios (no delay, both hosts 150 and 300 ms, the stylesheet alone 800 ms, the font files alone 1500 and 3000 ms) showed the four faces still loading whenever the first row becomes visible, because the stylesheet blocks the app's script, and the description already at 72.39 px when `document.fonts.ready` resolves, with 1.16 to 1.63 px of movement left in two scenarios from the demo-enter animation; the row's three candidate causes ruled out with captured state (scrollTop 1003 in every failure; the row under the pointer active; failures on interior full rows, both edge rows activating in all 266 passing runs); scope one file, trailer byte-exact, no CR; lint (426 files), typecheck, build, the spec with retries 0 all green. The fix round (1d492cfc) is a verbatim four-line comment replacement; the orchestrator checked it itself against the verifier's required text (the diff is those four lines re-wrapped to the file's width, Biome exit 0, the lefthook lint and typecheck green at commit, the tree identical before and after the reword), so no second verifier pass was run. The haiku-worker committed with a Haiku trailer; the orchestrator reworded that unpushed commit to the house trailer (f8d09aad became 1d492cfc, tree unchanged): the one amendment of this cluster, on a commit that had never left the host.

**Rulings.**
- **RA-82 closes with the cause, not the candidates.** The row's fix text listed three candidate races; none was the race. The FIXED note names the real one and pins the e2e case (`Tests: tests/e2e/virtualization.spec.ts::CE-119: hovering a row already in view does not scroll the virtual list`).
- **The worker's report was wrong twice, and the verifier caught both.** The report named the page header as the element that reflows (and read the nav wrapping onto two lines as the post-swap state; it wraps before the swap too), and it claimed its Vite dev server was gone when `node.exe` 93632 (vite.js from C:/w/cmdk-17-c7), its `cmd.exe` parent 74116 and the Bash shell 42256 were alive and answering on 5173: the worker had recorded a Git Bash process number (R/c7/vite.pid 1600937), not a Windows PID, so its `taskkill` found nothing, and the socket showed FIN_WAIT_1 rather than LISTENING when it looked. With `reuseExistingServer: !isCI` that server would have served the C7 tree, then a deleted tree, to any local e2e run on this host. The orchestrator verified the three command lines and stopped 93632 by PID (its parents had already exited), then confirmed 5173 answers on neither loopback address before any further Playwright run. Rule for every brief that starts a server from now on: capture the Windows PID (`netstat -ano` or PowerShell `Start-Process -PassThru`), stop it by that PID, and prove the port closed with a request before reporting (memory: no-image-name-process-kills).
- **The orchestrator's own first draft of this row repeated the worker's mechanism** (header reflow, 20 px, the pointer on the search input) and asserted that the spec's other describes read geometry and race the swap; the verifier found no other coordinate-aimed read (the Large Item Set poll at :262-263 compares a row against its list and retries; `list.hover()` at :452 and basic.spec.ts:144 go through actionability checks; :93 and :323 compare text and counts). Both claims were corrected before the row was appended; TEB-104 below is the corrected scope.
- **TEB-104 (orchestrator; TEST, low, 1.8.0, OPEN).** Every e2e run depends on fonts.googleapis.com and fonts.gstatic.com answering, the RA-82 wait is a per-describe workaround that a new coordinate-aimed case in any spec would have to repeat, and after `fonts.ready` the layout still moves 1.16 to 1.63 px in some loads from the `demo-enter` animation (styles.css:674-680), inside the re-read margin but not zero. PG-70 (OPEN 1.8.0, PERF: the render-blocking cross-origin stylesheet) records the same stylesheet from the playground's side. The fix: self-host the fonts or use `font-display: optional` under PG-70, or abort the two hosts in a shared e2e fixture; `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` in the Playwright `use` block; then drop the per-describe wait. Acceptance: the suite green with the font hosts blocked, and the hover case without its wait green across delayed fonts.
- **RA-82's row keeps its finding line.** The row points at :542 as found; after the fix the test starts at :497 and the assertion sits at :546. The FIXED note pins the case by title, which the claims convention already prefers to a line.
- **CI retries hide this class.** playwright.config.ts keeps `retries: isCI ? 2 : 0`. The policy stands (SEC-72 counts a job as working once green, and a retried pass is not evidence), and the local loops with retries 0 are the instrument that finds the class; nothing in CI changes here.

**The landing.** S/land-cluster-17.sh c7 1d492cfc 2 2e3e05e3 S/commit-ledger-c7.txt from the main checkout (R/c7/land-chain.log; the script, the ledger script and the message file copied to R/tools/). Preconditions held: the main checkout on release/1.7.0 at 2e3e05e3, clean apart from the owner's untracked AGENTS.md; 1.7/c7 at 1d492cfc, two commits over 2e3e05e3, based on it, both carrying the house trailer (the script checks the trailer of every commit it lands). Fast-forward to 1d492cfc; frozen install rc 0; the sweep (below); build rc 0; size-limit rc 0 with the RC rows unchanged (core 7742 B of 7840, react 6491 of 6590, react/dialog 2104 of 2200, CommandSeparator 293 of 390, core + react 12038 of 12130: no source file changed); S/c7-ledger.mjs (TEB-104 added OPEN for 1.8.0 from S/row-teb-104.json; RA-82 FIXED at f5b02ee3..1d492cfc, fixedBy f5b02ee, with the cause, the counts from worker and verifier, the three candidate causes ruled out and the pin Tests: tests/e2e/virtualization.spec.ts::CE-119: hovering a row already in view does not scroll the virtual list; RA-82 is a TEST row, so the BUG matrix is untouched); the matrix test gate green (261 tests); the ledger commit e73b0c3d (docs/ledger/findings.json only, 6 insertions and 5 deletions). The worker's orphaned Vite server was stopped and 5173 confirmed closed before the chain ran; the chain itself runs no Playwright. Then S/push-17.sh pushed e73b0c3d and watched CI (below). Every brief, report, findings file, trace and probe of the cluster is under R/c7/ (brief.md, report.md, verifier-brief.md, verifier-findings.md, runs.md, run-1 to run-3 with the failing trace, fixrun-4 to -6, probe.mjs, probe2.mjs, the probe-d* logs, the gate logs, land-chain.log, push.log).
